## Claims

### CLM-C01-01: Symbol counts are the complete invariant of the commutation congruence

- **cycle:** C01
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (commEq_iff_count, projection_is_unary); standard axioms only
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  Two words over a finite alphabet are congruent modulo the swap relations xy = yx iff every symbol occurs
  equally often in both; the projection of a word onto a symbol s is the unary word s...s of that length.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C01.md#theorem-1-clm-c01-01--counting-is-the-complete-invariant-of-commutation
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C01_Quantity.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C01-01
- **novelty_basis:** Free commutative monoid on Sigma is N^Sigma (Parikh vectors, Parikh 1966); special case of Mazurkiewicz trace monoids (Cartier-Foata 1969).
- **applications:** Linear-time decision of equality modulo commutation; justifies introducing N in System C.

### CLM-C01-02: One symbol, one relation a^m = a^(m+p) gives exactly m+p classes (index m, period p)

- **cycle:** C01
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (gen_iff, exactly_m_add_p_classes); standard axioms only
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  On one-symbol words, a^x ~ a^y modulo a^m = a^(m+p) (p >= 1) iff nf(x) = nf(y) with nf(x) = x for x < m and
  m + ((x - m) mod p) otherwise; there are exactly m + p classes.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C01.md#theorem-2-clm-c01-02--one-symbol-one-relation-index-and-period
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C01_Quantity.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C01-02
- **novelty_basis:** Index and period of monogenic semigroups (e.g. Howie 1995, Fundamentals of Semigroup Theory).

### CLM-C01-03: Several one-symbol relations have index min m_i and period gcd(n_i - m_i)

- **cycle:** C01
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C01.md, Theorem 3), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md); computationally corroborated on 6159 relation sets and (Skeptic) 4000 random sets
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The congruence on {a}* generated by a^(m_i) = a^(n_i) (m_i < n_i, i = 1..r) identifies a^x and a^y iff
  x = y or (x, y >= M and P | x - y), where M = min m_i and P = gcd(n_i - m_i); it has M + P classes.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C01.md#theorem-3-clm-c01-03--one-symbol-several-relations
- **evidence:** EXP-C01-02
- **novelty_basis:** Standard description of finitely generated congruences on (N,+) / cyclic semigroups.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C01-04: Multiplication emerges as composition of substitutions; with two symbols it is non-commutative

- **cycle:** C01
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C01.md, Theorem 4), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md); exhaustive check of all laws on a^0..a^12 (EXP-C01-03)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  (End({a}*), pointwise juxtaposition, composition) is a commutative semiring isomorphic to (N, +, x) via
  phi -> |phi(a)|. For two symbols modulo commutation, composition of endomorphisms is not commutative.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C01.md#theorem-4-clm-c01-04--multiplication-as-composition-of-transformations
- **evidence:** EXP-C01-03
- **novelty_basis:** Endomorphism semiring of (N,+) is (N,+,x); endomorphisms of N^2 are 2x2 matrices over N.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C01-05: H-C01-4: a single rearranging relation generates counting equivalence on long words (refuted)

- **cycle:** C01
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For every relation u <-> v over {a,b} with u != v and equal symbol counts, the generated congruence
  coincides with counting (Parikh) equivalence on all words of length >= |u|.

- **evidence:** EXP-C01-04
- **counterexample:** u = aab, v = aba: the word baa is fixed by every rewrite, so its class {baa} is smaller than its counting class {aab, aba, baa}. All 33 relations of length 3-4 fail (EXP-C01-04).
- **novelty_basis:**

  The negative answer is implied by the Golod-Shafarevich/Anick lower bound H >= 1/(1 - qx + x^k) for one-relator algebras (Anick 1982; Piontkovski 1999) - a single relation cannot make the classes as coarse as counting classes. Historian audit, reviews/historian_C01_C02.md.

- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T08:13:57Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C01; Historian novelty audit 2026-10-18 (reviews/historian_C01_C02.md))

### CLM-C01-06: Border-containment theorem for one length-preserving relation

- **cycle:** C01
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C01.md, Theorem 5), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md); Lean covers only the list identities of the one-step critical-pair join (common_border_joinable) - the overlap analysis, Newman's lemma and Guibas-Odlyzko are not formalised; corroborated by 756 + 1632 relations and (Skeptic) local confluence of 168,936 containment pairs

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  Let u != v be words of length k over a q-letter alphabet with Borders(u) contained in Borders(v). Then
  {u -> v} is convergent, the classes of length-n words modulo u = v correspond to the length-n words
  avoiding u, and the class-count generating function is c_u(x) / (x^k + (1 - q x) c_u(x)), c_u the
  autocorrelation polynomial of u. For unbordered u this is 1/(1 - q x + x^k); for q = 2 the counts are
  partial sums of (k-1)-bonacci numbers (k = 3: F(n+3) - 1).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C01.md#theorem-5-clm-c01-06--the-border-containment-theorem
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C01_Quantity.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C01-04; EXP-C01-05; EXP-C01-07
- **novelty_basis:**

  Length-preserving special case of the one-rule confluence criterion of W. Kurth, Some combinatorial properties of complete semi-Thue systems, Sem. Lothar. Combin. B18m (1987) https://www.mat.univie.ac.at/~slc/opapers/s18kurth.html (generalising Book 1983 and F. Otto, C. Wrathall, Math. Systems Theory 18 (1985) 135-143, https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01699465; see also C. Wrathall, LNCS 572 (1992) 237-246), combined with L. J. Guibas, A. M. Odlyzko, JCTA 30 (1981) 183-208. Kurth's criterion is an iff; the converse is CLM-C01-11. The Lean overlap lemma is a verification asset, not new mathematics.

- **applications:** Exact information content of words modulo an invisible local rewrite (e.g. log2(phi) bits/symbol modulo aab = aba).
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T08:13:57Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C01; Historian novelty audit 2026-10-18 (reviews/historian_C01_C02.md, section 2))

The method (finite completion, automaton of normal words, rational series) is Ufnarovski's framework (Encycl. Math. Sci. 57, 1995).

### CLM-C01-08: Common-border conjecture: one-relation class counts depend only on common borders

- **cycle:** C01
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For all words u != v of equal length k over a q-letter alphabet, the number of congruence classes of
  length-n words modulo u = v has generating function c(x) / (x^k + (1 - q x) c(x)), where
  c(x) = 1 + sum over words b that are borders of both u and v of x^(k - |b|).

- **evidence:** EXP-C01-05; EXP-C01-06; EXP-C01-07
- **novelty_basis:**

  Novel only in the case 'common border without containment'. The case with no common border (c = 1) follows from R. D. Gray, B. Steinberg, Forum Math. Sigma 14 (2026) e58, Thm 8.1 (https://doi.org/10.1017/fms.2026.10209) with Anick's strong freeness, and for different first/last letters from M. Albenque, P. Nadeau, FPSAC 2009 Cor. 3 + Adjan (CLM-C01-09); the containment case is Kurth 1987 (CLM-C01-06). The homological counterpart of the common-border dichotomy is Gray-Steinberg, Selecta Math. 28 (2022) art. 59, Thm B (https://doi.org/10.1007/s00029-022-00773-3). Searched without finding the growth formula: Anick, Piontkovski, Iyudu-Shkarin, Ufnarovski, Cain-Gray-Malheiro, Nyberg-Brodda survey, Rigo, OEIS (Historian audit).

- **limitations:**

  Case c = 1 PROVEN from the literature (CLM-C01-09, deduction checked by the Skeptic); containment PROVEN (CLM-C01-06); exactly one common border proved modulo one injectivity lemma (CLM-C01-10); two or more common borders without containment open. Skeptic search (reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md): 295,358 relation orbits (binary k <= 11, alphabets up to 6 letters, n up to 27) and 1,491 targeted relations with 1-4 common borders, 0 failures.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C01-09: Common-border formula when u and v share no border (c = 1) - consequence of published results

- **cycle:** C01
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  derivation from published theorems (Gray-Steinberg 2026 Thm 8.1 / 2022 Thm 3.3(2)(i); Anick 1982) written by the Historian (reviews/historian_C01_C02.md section 2); every step checked against the sources by the Skeptic (reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md section (d), steps 1a-1d); relies on the cited theorems, not re-proved

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  If u != v have length k over a q-letter alphabet and no word is a border of both, the class counts of length-n
  words modulo u = v have generating function 1/(1 - q x + x^k).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C01.md#theorem-8-clm-c01-09--the-case-without-common-borders-a-corollary-of-published-theorems
- **evidence:** EXP-C01-06; EXP-C01-07; EXP-C01-08
- **novelty_basis:**

  Derived by the Historian from Gray-Steinberg 2026 Thm 8.1 (no common border => Hochschild cohomological dimension <= 2 => gl.dim K<A>/(u-v) <= 2 => u - v strongly free, Anick 1982 => Hilbert series 1/(1-qx+x^k)); for different first or last letters also Albenque-Nadeau 2009 Cor. 3 + Adjan's cancellativity. All 527 relations left open by EXP-C01-07 are in this case.

- **limitations:** Relies on the published theorems of Gray-Steinberg and Anick (not re-proved by the lab). Settles all 527 relations left open by EXP-C01-07 (527/527 have c = 1, re-checked by the Skeptic).
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T08:39:48Z verification: COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED → PROVEN (sync:C01; Skeptic check of the Historian's derivation 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md (d)) - every step upheld; upgraded to PROVEN)

### CLM-C01-10: Common-border formula with exactly one common border (derivation sketch from Gray-Steinberg 2022)

- **cycle:** C01
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  If u != v of length k share exactly one common border z (neither border set containing the other), the class
  counts have generating function c/(x^k + (1 - q x) c) with c = 1 + x^(k - |z|).

- **evidence:** EXP-C01-06; EXP-C01-07; EXP-C01-08
- **novelty_basis:**

  Historian derivation (reviews/historian_C01_C02.md, section 2 (C)): identities (P) H = G_y + x^(k-|y|) G_z and (E) H(1 - qx) + x^k G_z = 1 read off Gray-Steinberg 2022 Thm 6.6 and Props 8.3, 8.5 (G_w = growth series of the left ideal M[w]); for y = z they give the formula. No published statement of the growth formula found.

- **limitations:**

  Proved modulo one lemma - injectivity of b -> b([u'_-] - [v'_-]) on ZM[z], asserted but not proved in the Historian's derivation (Skeptic, reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md (d)(3c)); identities (P), (E) and the injectivity premise hold numerically (3638 (P)-terms, 5648 (E)-terms, 2591 injectivity instances on 272 relations, 0 failures); verified for the 12 lab relations for all n by finite completion.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C01-11: Converse of border containment: u -> v (|u| = |v|) is confluent iff Borders(u) is contained in Borders(v)

- **cycle:** C01
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:** For words u != v of equal length, the one-rule system {u -> v} is confluent if and only if every border of u is a border of v.
- **evidence:** EXP-C01-08
- **novelty_basis:**

  Kurth (1987) proves the iff for one-rule systems with the chain condition (length-preserving case); not re-proved by the lab. EXP-C01-08 finds no mismatch among 28,850 ordered pairs (binary k <= 7, ternary k <= 4); the Historian's scratch run found none among 152,928.


### CLM-C02-01: Integer bases emerge as normal forms of the exchange rule b[k] -> [k+1]

- **cycle:** C02
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C02.md Theorem 2), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md); exhaustive check of 134 derivation graphs and n <= 20000; Skeptic enumerated all derivations for b = 2..7, 360 cases

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  From n tokens at level 0, every maximal derivation of the rule "b tokens at level k -> 1 token at level k+1"
  (b >= 2) is finite, ends at the base-b expansion of n, and has exactly (n - s_b(n))/(b - 1) steps.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C02.md#theorem-2-clm-c02-01--integer-bases
- **evidence:** EXP-C02-01
- **novelty_basis:** Chip-firing on a path / standard carrying; the step count sum_i floor(n/b^i) = (n - s_b(n))/(b - 1) is Legendre's identity.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C02-02: Abelian property of carry rules (order of exchanges is irrelevant)

- **cycle:** C02
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C02.md Theorem 1), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md); induction cited from Bjorner-Lovasz-Shor; 1500 random rules, 0 violations
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For a carry rule m[k] -> sum_j b_j [k+j], one-step firings at different levels commute. Hence from any
  configuration either all maximal derivations are infinite or all are finite with the same endpoint,
  length and firing vector.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C02.md#theorem-1-clm-c02-02--the-abelian-property
- **evidence:** EXP-C02-01; EXP-C02-04
- **novelty_basis:** Strong convergence of chip-firing (Bjorner-Lovasz-Shor 1991); abelian sandpile (Dhar 1990); abelian networks (Bond-Levine).
- **status history:**

### CLM-C02-03: Termination dichotomy for carry rules away from sum b = m

- **cycle:** C02
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C02.md Theorem 3), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  If sum_j b_j < m every derivation from every configuration terminates. If sum_j b_j > m, the emergent base
  satisfies r < 1 and no derivation from n tokens at level 0 terminates when n > (m - 1)/(1 - r).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C02.md#theorem-3-clm-c02-03--termination-dichotomy-away-from-b--m
- **evidence:** EXP-C02-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  Folklore monovariants (token count; conserved value): chip-firing with a sink (Bjorner-Lovasz, J. Algebraic Combin. 1 (1992) 305-328); half-line analogue of the halting criterion of Bond-Levine, Abelian networks II, Selecta Math. 22 (2016) 319-340; Tanton's Exploding Dots machines 'fire off to infinity'. Not found verbatim, but a two-line consequence of standard arguments.

- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T08:13:57Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C02; Historian novelty audit 2026-10-18 (reviews/historian_C01_C02.md, section 3))

### CLM-C02-04: Carry rules with sum b = m never terminate from all unary starts (uniform glider)

- **cycle:** C02
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  two independent written proofs - proofs/paper/C02.md Theorem 9 (spectral, by the Director) and Theorem 10 (Perron-Frobenius, by the Skeptic, checked line by line by the Director/Formal Verifier 2026-10-18); corroborated by EXP-C02-03, EXP-C02-07 and the Skeptic's simulation of 668,793 aperiodic critical rules

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For every carry rule with sum_j b_j = m there is a unary start n whose sweep reaches a window dominating the
  uniform-firing glider T = (T_1, ..., T_J), T_i = sum_{j >= i} b_j (if g = gcd{j : b_j > 0} > 1: the glider T'
  of the reduced rule b'_i = b_(gi) on the levels g*N); consequently its derivations from n are infinite. In fact
  every unary start n >= t*·m has this property (Theorems 9-10).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C02.md#theorem-9-clm-c02-11--critical-rules-never-terminate-from-large-starts
- **evidence:** EXP-C02-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  Closest prior work - J. Propp, 'How do you write one hundred in base 3/2?' (2017, endnote 3) proposes the family m -> p(x) with no results; Borodin et al., Minnesota J. Undergrad. Math. 6 (2021) (two-directional firing, always terminating); Bond-Levine 2016 (critical case, finite networks only). No statement of the uniform glider found (low-medium confidence).

- **limitations:** Theorem 10 gives an explicit but crude bound t* (via a Wielandt exponent); observed n0 <= 125 for the 98 rules of EXP-C02-07; largest first gliding t found by the Skeptic is 2744.
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T08:13:57Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → POTENTIALLY NOVEL (sync:C02; Historian novelty audit 2026-10-18 (reviews/historian_C01_C02.md, section 3))
  - 2026-10-18T08:20:07Z verification: COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED → PROVEN (sync:C02; Proved by the Director (Theorem 9, spectral argument) after the Historian audit marked it the only unpublished C02 result)

### CLM-C02-07: Uniqueness of representations when the reduced carry polynomial is primitive and irreducible

- **cycle:** C02
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib for the polynomial core (digits_unique, exists_large_coeff; standard axioms only); reduction to the lattice and the converse by written proof, skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md); statement restricted after the review (the converse fails for 2[k] -> 4[k+1], existence fails for 2[k] -> 3[k+1])

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  Let g = gcd{j : b_j > 0} and P~(y) = sum_i b_(gi) y^i - m. If P~ is primitive and irreducible over Q, then
  distinct stable configurations supported on multiples of g have distinct values; in particular, if
  sum_j b_j < m, every n has exactly one stable representation there, namely rep(n). If P~ is not primitive
  and sum_j b_j <= m (more generally every b_(gi) < m*d, d the content of P~), two such configurations with
  equal value exist.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C02.md#theorem-7-clm-c02-07--uniqueness-of-representations
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C02_Exchange.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C02-05
- **novelty_basis:**

  In substance S. Akiyama, C. Frougny, J. Sakarovitch, Israel J. Math. 168 (2008) 53-91, Lemma 5 (the same Gauss-lemma and constant-term argument for qX - p) transplanted to sum b_j y^j - m; general framework K. Scheicher, P. Surer, J. Thuswaldner, C. van de Woestijne, Digit systems over commutative rings, IJNT 10 (2014) 1459-1483; monic ancestor Kovacs-Petho (1991). The Lean formalisation is a verification asset.

- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T08:13:57Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C02; Historian novelty audit 2026-10-18 (reviews/historian_C01_C02.md, section 3))

### CLM-C02-08: Exchange representations respect the radix order

- **cycle:** C02
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C02.md Theorem 6, gap closed by the highest-firing-level argument), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md); 0 inversions for 31 rules up to n = 3000 and (Skeptic, LIFO order) 448 rules up to n = 1500

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:** For every carry rule with sum b < m and every n, rep(n) < rep(n+1) in radix order.

- **evidence:** EXP-C02-03
- **novelty_basis:** In substance AFS 2008 Prop. 11 (radix order agrees with numerical order for rational bases) via the odometer argument; the general rule was not found verbatim.

### CLM-C02-09: The Fibonacci exchange system terminates at the Zeckendorf representation; shortest derivation n - z(n)

- **cycle:** C02
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C02.md Theorem 8), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C01_C02.md); exhaustive check of 2167 configurations (Skeptic - 6617, own code)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  With weights 1,2,3,5,8,..., the exchanges [k]+[k+1] -> [k+2], 2[k] -> [k+1]+[k-2] (k >= 2), 2[1] -> [2]+[0],
  2[0] -> [1] terminate from every configuration at the Zeckendorf representation of its value, and the
  shortest derivation from n tokens at level 0 has n - z(n) steps (z(n) = number of Zeckendorf terms).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C02.md#theorem-8-clm-c02-09--the-fibonacci-exchange-system-zeckendorf-game
- **evidence:** EXP-C02-02; EXP-C02-06
- **novelty_basis:** The Zeckendorf game (Baird-Smith, Epstein, Flint, Miller, arXiv:1809.04881) - termination and shortest game N - Z(N); OEIS A219641.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C02-11: Termination classification of carry rules: terminating from every unary start iff sum b < m

- **cycle:** C02
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proofs (proofs/paper/C02.md Theorem 3, skeptic-reviewed; Theorems 9 and 10 - two independent proofs of the critical case, Theorem 10 by the Skeptic and checked by the Director); 98 critical rules checked for all starts <= 2000 (EXP-C02-07)

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  A carry rule m[k] -> sum_j b_j [k+j] terminates from every unary start iff sum_j b_j < m. If sum_j b_j = m,
  every derivation from n tokens at level 0 is infinite for all sufficiently large n (the sweep window converges,
  up to bounded error, to a large multiple of the tail-sum vector T and then dominates T).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C02.md#theorem-9-clm-c02-11--critical-rules-never-terminate-from-large-starts
- **evidence:** EXP-C02-03; EXP-C02-07
- **novelty_basis:**

  Historian audit found no equivalent for the critical case (closest - Propp 2017 proposed the rule family; Bond-Levine, Abelian networks II (2016) prove the analogous critical-case non-halting for FINITE networks via the spectral radius; the half-line network here is infinite). The sum b < m and sum b > m halves are folklore (CLM-C02-03).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C03-01: A one-rule system that decreases the count of some letter terminates

- **cycle:** C03
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (terminating_of_count_lt); standard axioms only
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For any alphabet and rule l -> r, if some letter occurs fewer times in r than in l, every derivation is
  finite. In particular 0^p 1^q -> 1^r 0^s terminates whenever s < p or r < q.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C03.md#theorem-1-clm-c03-01--counting
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C03_Termination.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C03-01
- **novelty_basis:** Folklore measure argument; the termination status of the whole family is in Zantema-Geser (AAECC 2000).

### CLM-C03-02: Conserving rules terminate (s = p or r = q)

- **cycle:** C03

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  inversions drops by pq at each step.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C03.md#theorem-2-clm-c03-02--conservation

### CLM-C03-03: Self-embedding derivations prove non-termination; the doubling region r >= 2q, s >= 2p always loops

- **cycle:** C03
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (not_terminating_of_loop, not_terminating_double); standard axioms only
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  If u ->+ x u y then the rule is non-terminating. If r >= 2q and s >= 2p (p, q >= 1) then
  0^p 1^(2q) -> 1^r 0^s 1^q -> 1^r 0^(s-2p) (0^p 1^(2q)) 1^(r-2q) 0^s, so 0^p 1^q -> 1^r 0^s is non-terminating.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C03.md#theorem-3-clm-c03-03--loops-and-the-doubling-region
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C03_Termination.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C03-01; EXP-C03-02
- **novelty_basis:** Self-embedding (looping) is the standard non-termination criterion; the status of these rules is in Zantema-Geser (2000).

### CLM-C03-04: 011 -> 11100 is non-terminating although r < 2q

- **cycle:** C03
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (not_terminating_0_11; each step checked by decide); standard axioms only
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The rule 0 1 1 -> 1 1 1 0 0 (p = 1, q = 2, r = 3, s = 2) admits the loop
  001111 -> 01110011 -> 0111011100 -> 011111100100 -> 11100111100100 = 111 . 001111 . 00100.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C03.md#theorem-4-clm-c03-04--a-loop-with-r--2q
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C03_Termination.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C03-01
- **novelty_basis:** Status covered by Zantema-Geser (2000).

### CLM-C03-05: Lifting lemma - loops transfer along letter morphisms (explains divisibility)

- **cycle:** C03
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (step_map, loop_map); 147 + 225 lifted certificates re-checked (EXP-C03-03/04); mirror symmetry by written argument
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  If a substitution sigma maps l -> r to l' -> r' (sigma(l) = l', sigma(r) = r'), it maps every step and every
  loop of the first system to a step and a loop of the second. With sigma(0) = 0^p: a loop of
  0 1^q -> 1^r 0^k gives a loop of 0^p 1^q -> 1^r 0^(kp). Reverse-complement maps (p,q,r,s) to (q,p,s,r).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C03.md#theorem-5-clm-c03-05--substitution-lifting-lemma
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C03_Termination.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C03-03; EXP-C03-04
- **novelty_basis:** Closure of rewriting under morphisms is standard; its use to propagate loop certificates across the parameter family is a routine application.

### CLM-C03-10: Divisibility characterization of termination for 0^p 1^q -> 1^r 0^s (H-C03-4)

- **cycle:** C03
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  published theorem - Zantema-Geser Theorem 1.1 (tech. report UU-CS-1994-44, p. 1; AAECC 2000 Thm 1), statement read verbatim by the Historian; equivalence with the lab's formulation checked by case analysis (Historian, re-checked by the Director) and on all 1 <= p, q, r, s <= 40; not re-proved by the lab

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  0^p 1^q -> 1^r 0^s (p, q, r, s >= 1) is non-terminating iff (a) r >= 2q and s >= 2p, or (b) s >= 2p,
  q < r < 2q and p divides s, or (c) r >= 2q, p < s < 2p and q divides r. Otherwise it terminates.

- **proof:** reviews/historian_C03_C10.md#20-the-zantemageser-theorem-verbatim-statement-and-comparison-priority-item
- **evidence:** EXP-C03-02; EXP-C03-03; EXP-C03-04
- **novelty_basis:**

  Verbatim rediscovery of H. Zantema, A. Geser, A complete characterization of termination of 0^p 1^q -> 1^r 0^s, tech. report UU-CS-1994-44 (https://ics-archive.science.uu.nl/research/techreps/repo/CS-1994/1994-44.pdf), RTA 1995 (LNCS 914) and AAECC 11 (2000) 1-25, Theorem 1.1; the lab's three non-terminating cases match ZG's own list on p. 2 word for word (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).

- **limitations:**

  Proved for (a) in general and for (b)/(c) whenever the base rule 0 1^q -> 1^r 0^(s/p) has a known loop (all q <= 6, s/p <= 6; the Skeptic extended the base-family loops to q = 7); termination proved only for s <= p or r <= q. The 24 open rules with parameters <= 5 (all predicted terminating) include Zantema's problem. The loop certificates of the base-family and mirror rules are not stored in the results (reviews/skeptic_C03_C04_C06.md, C4); the Zantema-Geser (2000) characterisation, which would settle the conjecture, could not be retrieved (CLM-C20-04).

- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:10Z verification: COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED → PROVEN (sync:C03; Historian audit 2026-10-18 - identical to the published Zantema-Geser theorem; status PROVEN by literature)

### CLM-C04-03: The rules aab -> ababa and aab -> babaa (and their six symmetric images) terminate

- **cycle:** C04
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof in cycle C20 (proofs/paper/C20.md Theorems 1-2 - run encoding, journeys under the rightmost strategy, random descent); all words of length <= 12 verified (EXP-C20-01); awaiting independent skeptic review

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The one-rule systems aab -> ababa and aab -> babaa over {a,b} (and their images under reversal and
  letter exchange) are terminating.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C20.md#theorem-1-clm-c20-01--aab--ababa-terminates-and-dn--2n2--1-n--2
- **evidence:** EXP-C04-02; EXP-C20-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  Both rules are right barren in McNaughton's sense, and barren one-rule systems terminate (McNaughton; A. Geser, ICASE Report 2002-33, Thm 3.2-3.3); the lab's proof is independent and also gives the exact complexities (CLM-C20-01/02).

- **limitations:**

  All words of length <= 9 (aab -> ababa, maximal derivation 127 = 2^7 - 1) and <= 12 (aab -> babaa, maximal 62) reach normal forms; no linear interpretation (coefficients <= 7), weighted factor count (|f| <= 4) or self-embedding (seeds <= 10, depth 30) was found.

- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T16:49:32Z verification: COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED → PROVEN (sync:C04; Proved in cycle C20 (CLM-C20-01, CLM-C20-02); the Skeptic's review (which found no loop and no matrix interpretation) predates the proof)

### CLM-C04-04: ab -> ba: every derivation has inv(w) steps, D(n) = floor(n^2/4), P-positions = even inversion count

- **cycle:** C04
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib (inv_step, normal_iff_inv_zero, derivation_length_eq_inv, and p_positions = the kernel property of the even-inversion words; standard axioms only); the steps 'kernel = P-positions' (terminating game) and D(n) = max_k k(n-k) = floor(n^2/4) are written arguments (skeptic review 2026-10-18)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For the rule ab -> ba every step lowers the number of inversions by exactly one; the normal forms are the
  words b^i a^j; every complete derivation from w has exactly inv(w) steps; the derivational complexity is
  floor(n^2/4); and in the rewriting game the P-positions are exactly the words with an even number of
  inversions.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C04.md#theorem-5-clm-c04-04--the-rule-ab--ba
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C04_Derivations.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C04-03; EXP-C04-07
- **novelty_basis:** Classical (bubble-sort inversions); extremal case of Metivier's conjecture proved by Bertrand (TCS 123, 1994); cited as the quadratic example in derivational-complexity literature (Kobayashi).
- **status history:**

- **cycle:** C04
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (random_descent, unbordered_diamond, unbordered_random_descent; standard axioms only)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  (a) Abstractly: if every local peak b <- a -> c is joinable by two derivations of equal length, then whenever
  a reaches a normal form b in n steps, every derivation from a has at most n steps and every derivation from a
  to a normal form has exactly n steps and ends in b. (b) For any rule l -> r over any alphabet with l
  unbordered, two distinct one-step reducts of a word have a common one-step reduct; hence (a) applies.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C04.md#theorem-4-clm-c04-05--unbordered-left-hand-sides-give-random-descent
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C04_Derivations.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C04-05
- **novelty_basis:**

  Local characterisation of random descent - V. van Oostrom, "Random descent", RTA 2007, LNCS 4533, 314-328 (Isabelle formalisation in arXiv:1802.08437); one-step diamond lemma for non-overlapping redexes is classical (Newman 1942). The Lean development here is an independent formalisation.


### CLM-C04-06: Every one-rule system has a constant-step potential; for terminating ones confluence is equivalent to random descent

- **cycle:** C04
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C04.md Theorem 2), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C03_C04_C06.md); abstract consequence and the length-changing case Lean-verified (steps_length_unique, length_changing_unique); potential checked on 5365 rules / 470,927 steps

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For every rule l -> r (l != r) over a finite alphabet there are Phi : words -> Z and delta != 0 with
  Phi(v) = Phi(u) + delta for every step u -> v (Phi = length if |l| != |r|; otherwise
  Phi(w) = sum of i^K over positions i carrying a suitable letter c, K the least exponent at which the power
  sums of the c-positions of l and r differ). Consequently all derivations between two given words have the same
  number of steps, and a terminating one-rule system is confluent iff it has random descent.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C04.md#theorem-2-clm-c04-06--every-one-rule-system-has-a-constant-step-potential
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C04_Derivations.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C04-05; EXP-C04-06
- **novelty_basis:** In substance - M. Latteux, Y. Roos 2014, Lemma 3.7; Y. Metivier 1985, Lemma 3.5; Adinayev-Stein 2019 (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).
- **notes:**

  Length uniformity alone is strictly weaker than random descent - 126 bordered length-preserving rules with |l| = 4, 5 are length-uniform on words <= 10 but not confluent (e.g. aaaba -> baabb on aaabaaaba).

- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:10Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C04; Historian audit 2026-10-18 (reviews/historian_C03_C10.md))

### CLM-C04-07: H-C04-1: random descent iff the left-hand side is unbordered (refuted)

- **cycle:** C04

- **novelty_basis:** One line from van Oostrom (RTA 2007) (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).

### CLM-C04-08: Rewriting games of random-descent rules are parity games (Grundy value = D(w) mod 2)

- **cycle:** C04
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C04.md Theorem 9, induction on D); the instance ab -> ba is Lean-verified (p_positions); checked on all 102 RD rules and all words of length <= 10
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  If a one-rule system has random descent, the Sprague-Grundy value of its rewriting game at w is D(w) mod 2, D(w) the common length of all maximal derivations from w; the P-positions are the words with even D(w).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C04.md#theorem-9-clm-c04-08--parity-for-random-descent-games
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C04_Derivations.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C04-07
- **novelty_basis:** Elementary combinatorial game theory - a game in which every play has the same length is decided by parity.

### CLM-C04-09: The rule aab -> ba has derivational complexity D(n) = n - 1 - min{k : 2^k + k >= n} (non-rational)

- **cycle:** C04
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C04.md Theorem 7), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C03_C04_C06.md); derivation lengths checked on all words of length <= 21; the arithmetic identity of proof steps (iv)-(v) checked to n = 20000

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For aab -> ba over {a,b}, the maximal derivation length over words of length n >= 1 is
  D(n) = n - 1 - kappa(n) with kappa(n) = min{k >= 0 : 2^k + k >= n}; D stalls exactly at n = 2^k + k + 1, and
  the generating function of D is not rational. The proof identifies the rule with the binary exchange system
  2[k] -> [k+1] (cycle C02) with an inert top level.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C04.md#theorem-7-clm-c04-09--aab--ba-has-complexity-n--log-n--o1
- **evidence:** EXP-C04-03; EXP-C04-04
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low confidence. Zantema-Geser Prop 3.2 gives only O(n); Kobayashi-Katsura-Shikishima-Tsuji (TCS 262, 2001) claim a complete complexity analysis of a family containing aab -> ba but were not read (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C04-10: Identity: OEIS A247983 (truncation index of the series for log 2) equals D(n+2) for aab -> ba

- **cycle:** C04
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C04.md Theorem 8, Jensen's inequality and a tail bound; valid for n >= 1), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C03_C04_C06.md); verified for 1 <= n <= 1500 with n + 80 bits and by the Skeptic for n <= 1200 with n + 120 bits

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  Let A(n) be the least k >= 1 with log 2 - sum_{h=1..k} 1/(h 2^h) < 2^-n (OEIS A247983, offset 1). Then for
  every n >= 1, A(n) = n - max{j >= 0 : 2^j + j <= n + 1}, and A(n) = D(n + 2) where D is the derivational
  complexity of the one-rule system aab -> ba. (At n = 0 the identity fails: A(0) = 1 but D(2) = 0.)

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C04.md#theorem-8-clm-c04-10--an-unexpected-identity-with-oeis-a247983
- **evidence:** EXP-C04-09; EXP-C04-10
- **novelty_basis:**

  Medium confidence. The identity answers the question left open by C. Kimberling in OEIS A247983 / A083706 (no formula given there); valid for n >= 1 (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C04-12: Complexity classes and closed forms of the 162 terminating rules with |l|, |r| <= 3

- **cycle:** C04
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The 162 terminating rules (55 symmetry classes) have derivational complexity linear (144 rules, of which 4
  are n - Theta(log n)), quadratic (14: floor(n^2/4), floor(n(n-1)/6), floor(n^2/8)) or exponential
  (4: 2^(n-1) - 1); for 54 of the 55 classes D(n) = floor((a n^2 + b n + c)/d) or 2^(n-1) - 1 on all computed
  terms.

- **evidence:** EXP-C04-03
- **novelty_basis:** Partial - Zantema-Geser 1994 section 3 (bubble sort, quadratic and exponential cases); Bertrand 1994; Kobayashi et al. 2001 (unread) (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).
- **limitations:** Closed forms are verified on n <= 12-20 only; proofs exist for ab -> ba (CLM-C04-04), the doubling rules (proofs/paper/C04.md Theorem 6) and aab -> ba (CLM-C04-09).
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:10Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C04; Historian audit 2026-10-18 (reviews/historian_C03_C10.md))

### CLM-C04-13: Rewriting games with lhs a^k are take-and-break games: aa -> b is Dawson's Kayles, aa -> ab is Officers

- **cycle:** C04
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C04.md Theorem 10), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C03_C04_C06.md); word games = run games for n <= 20 and = octal recursions for n <= 3000; decomposition checked on all words <= 10 for 58 rules

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For a terminating rule a^k -> u the rewriting game on w is the disjoint sum of games on the maximal a-runs,
  with explicit moves; in particular aa -> b (bb, bbb, bab) is the octal game 0.07 (Dawson's Kayles),
  aa -> ab (ba, abb, bba) is 0.6 (Officers), aaa -> b (bb, bbb, bab) is 0.007 (Treblecross), aaa -> ab
  (ba, abb, bba) is 0.06 and aaa -> aba is 0.4.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C04.md#theorem-10-clm-c04-13--unary-left-hand-sides-give-take-and-break-games
- **evidence:** EXP-C04-07; EXP-C04-08
- **novelty_basis:** Octal games are classically described as rows of pins (Berlekamp-Conway-Guy, Winning Ways); the rewriting presentation is a change of notation.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C04-16: Open: are the rewriting games of aa -> ab (Officers), aaa -> b (0.007) and aaa -> ab (0.06) ultimately periodic?

- **cycle:** C04
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-D
- **verification:** CONJECTURE

### CLM-C05-01: H-C05-1: a single identity t = x cannot restrict the orders of its finite models (refuted)

- **cycle:** C05
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** Every identity t = x has spectrum {1} or the set of all positive integers.
- **evidence:** EXP-C05-01
- **counterexample:**

  ETP law 167, x = (y*x)*(x*y): Lean-certified models of orders 4 and 5 (T4_law167, T5_law167) and no model of order 2, 3, 6 or 7 (law167_card_mod_four: every finite model has order 0 or 1 mod 4). The census EXP-C05-01 finds 61 of 115 nontrivial two-variable identities with spectra on n <= 6 that are neither {1} nor everything.


### CLM-C05-02: Spectrum census of the 115 nontrivial two-variable identities t = x with 2-4 operations (n <= 6, restricted ones to n <= 8)

- **cycle:** C05
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  Of the 115 identities t = x (x occurring in t, neither leftmost nor rightmost), 52 have models of every order
  1..6, 2 have none of orders 2..6, and 61 have restricted spectra; the 63 non-full ones fall into 15 patterns
  on n = 1..8 listed in research/cycles/C05_magma_spectra/README.md (e.g. 10111011 for 16 identities including
  the Dupont law, 10110010 = Mendelsohn pattern for law 66 and three equivalents, 1010001? for law 907).

- **evidence:** EXP-C05-01; EXP-C05-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  The Equational Theories Project classified all 741 classes of laws of order <= 4 and B. Le Floch's notes 'Spectrum of equational laws' (Lean Zulip upload spectrum.pdf) discuss every law with possibly non-full spectrum; our census agrees with every spectrum fragment stated there for the laws concerned.

- **limitations:** Non-memberships in the census are SAT-solver results (CaDiCaL) without proof certificates, except those re-certified in EXP-C05-06; one instance (law 167, n = 6) is UNKNOWN within budget.

### CLM-C05-03: H-C05-Q: nontrivial identities t = x force their finite models to be quasigroups (refuted)

- **cycle:** C05
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED

### CLM-C05-04: Law 167, x = (y*x)*(x*y): every finite model has order 0 or 1 mod 4

- **cycle:** C05
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (Infinity.C05.law167_card_mod_four; standard axioms only); models of orders 4 and 5 certified by decide
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  If a finite magma satisfies x = (y*x)*(x*y) for all x, y, its order n satisfies n = 0 or 1 (mod 4).
  (With the constructions of the ETP write-up "Spectra of order-3 equations" the spectrum is exactly
  {n : n = 0, 1 mod 4}.)

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C05.md#theorem-2-clm-c05-04--law-167-xyxxy-orders-are--0-1-mod-4
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C05_Magmas.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C05-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  Proposition 3 of the ETP write-up 'Spectra of order-3 equations' (Lean Zulip upload; attributed in Le Floch's notes to Ben Gunby-Mann) gives the same orbit argument and the full spectrum. Our addition is a Lean formalisation of the necessity half via a fixed-point-free involution on Sym2.


### CLM-C05-05: Identities x = y*s(x, y) make every left translation of a finite model bijective

- **cycle:** C05
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib for the x = y*s(x,y) case (left_mul_bijective_of_law); the general path version by written proof
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  If a finite magma satisfies x = y * s(x, y) for some binary term operation s, every left translation a -> y*a
  is a bijection (every row of the Cayley table is a permutation). More generally, in a finite model of t = x
  with x occurring once in t, every translation on the path from the root of t to x is a bijection.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C05.md#lemma-1-clm-c05-05--translations-along-the-path-to-a-unique-occurrence-of-x
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C05_Magmas.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C05-03
- **novelty_basis:** Standard finite-set argument (a surjective self-map of a finite set is bijective), used throughout the ETP notes.

### CLM-C05-06: H-C05-A: full spectrum iff affine models over every Z_n (refuted)

- **cycle:** C05
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** A nontrivial identity t = x has models of all orders 1..6 iff it has an affine model x*y = a x + b y + c over Z_n for every n <= 6.
- **evidence:** EXP-C05-05; EXP-C05-01
- **counterexample:**

  x = (y*x)*(x*(y*y)) has models of every order 1..6 (EXP-C05-01); of order 2 it is satisfied by NAND (Lean: nand2_law), yet no affine operation over Z_2 satisfies it (Lean: no_affine_model_mod2); its affine models exist only for n in {1, 5}. Three further identities behave alike (EXP-C05-05).


### CLM-C05-07: New spectrum memberships for open ETP laws: 677 has models of orders 5, 7, 9; 1286, 1313 and 1516 have models of order 9

- **cycle:** C05
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 kernel decide on explicit tables (E677_order5_law, E677_order7_law, E677_order9_law, E1286_order9_law, E1313_order9_law, E1516_order9_law; also E467_order9_law); only propext
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  Each of the following order-4 laws has a model of the stated order (explicit Cayley tables):
  677 x = y(x((yx)y)) - orders 5, 7, 9; 1286 x = y(((xy)x)y) - order 9; 1313 x = y(((yx)x)y) - order 9;
  1516 x = (yy)(x(xy)) - order 9. (A certified order-9 model of law 467, x = y(x(x(yy))), is also recorded, but
  that order is already covered by the notes' construction on Z[i]/(k+il) with k = 3, l = 0.)

- **proof:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C05_Magmas.lean (section Frontier)

Le Floch's notes 'Spectra of equational laws' (ETP, July 10, 2025), which list 1313 and 1516 as 'starting with' 1,5,7 and 1,5,7,8, 1286 as 'starting with 1,7', and give no sizes for 677. A second reading (2026-10-18) found that the notes' Gaussian-integer models of law 467 (all odd k^2 + l^2 with k, l of different parities) include 9 = 3^2 + 0^2, so 467 is not counted (the check is written out in proofs/paper/C05.md, Remark 5). The ETP paper (arXiv:2512.07087) defers detailed spectra to a forthcoming publication and was only partly retrieved; ETP model searches may already contain these models, so novelty is only relative to the sources read.

  3, 6, 7; 677 x = y(x((yx)y)) - orders 3, 4, 6; 883 x = y((xy)(yy)) - order 6; 1076 x = y((x(xy))y) - order 8;
  1083 x = y((x(yx))y) - orders 5, 6. Also certified, but already stated in the notes: no model of order 2 for
  501, 677, 1083 and 1719 x = (yy)((xx)y), and none of order 3 for 883.

The gaps at orders 3 and 4 are small enough that ETP finite-magma searches probably settle them; the plausible additions are the gaps at n >= 5 (501: 6, 7; 677: 6; 883: 6; 1076: 8; 1083: 5, 6).

### CLM-C06-01: Left-distributivity with a successor anchor: the Laver tables (rediscovery)

- **cycle:** C06
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  An anchored left-distributive magma (x*0 = x+1 mod N, x(yz) = (xy)(xz)) of size N exists exactly for
  N = 1, 2, 4, 8 among N <= 10 and is then unique (the Laver table A_n); Laver's recursion yields
  left-distributive tables exactly at the powers of 2 up to N = 64 (verified to N = 256 exhaustively).

- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C06_Laver.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C06-01; EXP-C06-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Published theorem - for every N there is a unique operation on {1..N} with p*1 = p+1 mod N and p*(q+1) = (p*q)*(p+1) for q < N (the recursion without its wrap-around instance), and it is left-distributive iff N is a power of 2 (R. Laver 1992/1995; elementary proof in A. Drapal's survey; see P. Dehornoy, Braids and Self-Distributivity (2000), Ch. X; P. Dehornoy, 'Some aspects of the SD-world', arXiv:1711.09792; P. Biane, arXiv:1810.00548). Lean checks A_3 and the non-existence for N = 3 (laver3_left_distributive, no_anchored_ld_three); the general theorem is not re-proved by the lab.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C06-02: Anchored associativity forces x*y = x + y + 1, for every N

- **cycle:** C06
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (anchored_assoc_unique; standard axioms only)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  If * on Z/N is associative and x*0 = x + 1 for all x, then x*y = x + y + 1; conversely this operation is associative and anchored. So every N has exactly one anchored associative magma (a cyclic group).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C06.md#theorem-1-clm-c06-02--anchored-associativity-gives-exactly-one-model-for-every-n
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C06_Laver.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C06-02
- **novelty_basis:** Elementary (the anchor is a right translation by a generator); folklore.

### CLM-C06-05: Anchored right-permutable magmas are x*y = x + c(y); there are N^(N-1) of them

- **cycle:** C06
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C06.md Theorem 4), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C03_C04_C06.md); counts 1, 2, 9, 64, 625 for N <= 5 (EXP-C06-02) and 7776 at N = 6 (Skeptic)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  If (xy)z = (xz)y on Z/N and x*0 = x + 1, then x*y = x + c(y) for a function c with c(0) = 1; conversely every such operation is right-permutable and anchored. Hence there are exactly N^(N-1) anchored right-permutable magmas of size N.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C06.md#theorem-4-clm-c06-05--anchored-right-permutable-magmas-x--y--x--cy-nn1-models
- **evidence:** EXP-C06-02
- **novelty_basis:** Partial, folklore level - centraliser of an N-cycle; count N^(N-1) (OEIS A000169) (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:10Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C06; Historian audit 2026-10-18 (reviews/historian_C03_C10.md))

### CLM-C06-06: An 'odd' law: (xy)z = x((yz)z) has an anchored model exactly for odd N, and it is unique

### CLM-C06-07: A 'golden' law: anchored models of (xy)z = x(y(zz)) correspond to roots of a^2 + a = 1 mod N

- **cycle:** C06
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C06.md Theorem 6), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C03_C04_C06.md); model counts equal the root counts for every N <= 12 (EXP-C06-04) and, by the Skeptic's complete enumeration, for N <= 17 and N = 19

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  The anchored magmas of size N satisfying (xy)z = x(y(zz)) are exactly x*y = x + 1 + a*y (mod N) with
  a^2 + a = 1 (mod N); distinct roots give distinct models. Hence the spectrum is the set of N = 5^e m with
  e in {0, 1} and every prime factor of m congruent to +-1 mod 5 (OEIS A089270: 1, 5, 11, 19, 29, 31, 41, 55, ...)
  and the number of models is the number of roots (OEIS A087781).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C06.md#theorem-6-clm-c06-07--a-golden-law-x--y--z--x--y--z--z
- **evidence:** EXP-C06-04
- **novelty_basis:**

  The number theory is classical (quadratic reciprocity and Hensel; OEIS A089270 and A087781 describe the moduli for which x^2 = x + 1 is solvable and count the roots, https://oeis.org/A089270, https://oeis.org/A087781). The correspondence between this magma law and the golden congruence was not found in the searches made (anchored-magma searches return only Laver tables).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C06-08: Anchored left-permutable magmas are commutative monoids in disguise (counted by OEIS A058156)

- **cycle:** C06
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib for the structural core (centralizer_eq, comp_eq, id_eq, leftPerm_assoc, leftPerm_comm, leftPerm_one_mul, leftPerm_mul_one; standard axioms only); the inverse construction is immediate (written proof proofs/paper/C06.md Theorem 7); counts agree for N <= 5 (EXP-C06-02)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  On Z/N, a.b := (a - 1)*b and x*y := (x + 1).y are inverse bijections between anchored magmas satisfying
  x(yz) = y(xz) and commutative monoid structures with identity 0. Hence the number of anchored left-permutable
  magmas of size N is the number of labelled commutative monoids of order N with a fixed identity (OEIS A058156:
  1, 2, 9, 94, 1486, ...).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C06.md#theorem-7-clm-c06-08--anchored-left-permutable-magmas-are-commutative-monoids-in-disguise
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C06_Laver.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C06-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Partial (downgraded) - a two-line lemma on left-permutable groupoids with a right identity; Dudek-Gigon 2013 (AG-groupoid version); OEIS A058156 (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).

- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:10Z novelty: POTENTIALLY NOVEL → REDISCOVERY (sync:C06; Historian audit 2026-10-18 - downgraded from POTENTIALLY NOVEL)

### CLM-C06-12: Anchored spectra are closed under coprime products

- **cycle:** C06
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C06.md Theorem 8), skeptic-reviewed 2026-10-18 (reviews/skeptic_C03_C04_C06.md); the Skeptic transported 9 actual model pairs (e.g. golden 5 x 11, odd 5 x 7) and all satisfy law and anchor

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For every set of laws, if anchored models of coprime sizes N and M exist, then an anchored model of size NM exists (transport the direct product along the Chinese remainder isomorphism, which maps x + 1 to (x + 1, x + 1)). In particular no anchored spectrum (which always contains 1) has the form {1} together with a set of primes containing two primes but not their product - for example {1} together with an infinite set of primes.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C06.md#theorem-8-clm-c06-12--anchored-spectra-are-closed-under-coprime-products
- **evidence:** EXP-C06-03; EXP-C06-04
- **novelty_basis:** In substance - closure of varieties under products; spectra of varieties (Gratzer; Froemke-Quackenbush 1975) (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:10Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C06; Skeptic review 2026-10-18 - proof upheld; consequence reworded ({1} together with primes))

### CLM-C07-01: The associative mex operations are 0, projections, min, +, XOR, nim-product and four relatives

- **cycle:** C07
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof for the 11 canonical formulas (proofs/paper/C07.md Theorem 1(a), induction using Conway's mex lemmas); computer-assisted for non-associativity (explicit violating triples in [0,64)^3 for 468 specs, exact tables cross-checked against a plain implementation); awaiting independent skeptic review

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  In the family of 512 mex operations (options f(a',b) [L], f(a,b') [R] and XOR combinations of f(a',b),
  f(a,b'), f(a',b') [D]), the canonical specs empty, L, R, D[x], D[y], D[z], L+R, D[x^y^z], L+R+D[z],
  D[x]+D[y], D[x]+D[y]+D[z] define for all a, b the operations 0, a, b, a[b>0], b[a>0], min(a,b), a XOR b,
  a (x) b (nim-product), a + b, ((a-1) XOR (b-1)) + 1 and a + b - 1 (the last two with 0 absorbing), all
  associative; 468 of the 512 specs are not associative; the remaining 33 specs agree with one of the 11
  operations on [0,64)^2.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C07.md#theorem-1-clm-c07-01--the-associative-mex-operations
- **evidence:** EXP-C07-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  Each formula is classical or elementary - nim-sum and nim-product (Conway, On Numbers and Games, 1976); a + b and min(a, b) are the Grundy functions of 'reduce either or both heaps' and 'reduce both heaps' (rank functions of product posets). The census of a 512-member family and the resulting classification were not found (one web search, OEIS); small.

- **limitations:** Agreement of the 33 non-canonical associative-looking specs with their formulas is verified on [0,64)^2 only.

### CLM-C07-05: The Jacobsthal row: row 2 of f(a,b) = mex{f(a',b) XOR f(a,b')} swaps the blocks [2^k - J_k, 2^k) and [2^k, 2^k + J_k)

- **cycle:** C07
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C07.md Theorem 3, induction over Jacobsthal blocks using J_(k+1) + J_k = 2^k and bit estimates); formula verified for all b < 2^15 by an independent bitset implementation (EXP-C07-03); awaiting independent skeptic review

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For the mex operation D[x^y], f(a,b) = mex{f(a',b) XOR f(a,b') : a' < a, b' < b}: f(0,b) = 0, f(1,b) = b, and
  g(b) = f(2,b) satisfies g(0) = 0 and, for every k >= 1, g(b) = b + J_k for 2^k - J_k <= b < 2^k and
  g(b) = b - J_k for 2^k <= b < 2^k + J_k, where J_k = (2^k - (-1)^k)/3 are the Jacobsthal numbers. Equivalently,
  g(b) is the least v with v and v XOR b both outside {g(b') : b' < b}. The row is an involution and is not
  additively periodic.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C07.md#theorem-3-clm-c07-05--the-jacobsthal-row
- **evidence:** EXP-C07-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  OEIS: no match for the row (2, 1, 4, 3, 8, 9, 10, 5, ...), for |g(b) - b| or for g(b) XOR b; text searches for greedy XOR-avoiding permutations found unrelated entries (A109812, A357578); a web search found no mex recursion of this form (closest: Multiplicative Modular Nim, arXiv:2507.08830, a different recursion). Small.


### CLM-C07-06: H-C07-4: every row of every mex operation is eventually additively periodic (refuted)

- **cycle:** C07
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** For every operation of the family and every a, the row b -> f(a,b) satisfies f(a, b + p) = f(a, b) + s for all large b, for some p >= 1 and s, as for Wythoff's game.

### CLM-C08-01: Every shifted Knuth product a o_t b = sum F(k+l+t) over Zeckendorf indices is associative (t >= 0)

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib (Infinity.C08.circ_assoc, circ_comm, key, window; standard axioms only) for all t >= 0; counterexamples for s = 0, 1 by computation; no failure in 20,000 random triples up to 1e9 (t = 0)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For every t >= 0 the operation a o_t b = sum_{k in Z(a)} sum_{l in Z(b)} F_(k+l+t) on the natural numbers
  (Z(n) the Zeckendorf index set, indices >= 2) is associative and commutative; in the lab's G-indexing these
  are the shifts s = t + 2 >= 2. For s = 0 and s = 1 associativity fails ((2 o 4) o 4 != 2 o (4 o 4) for s = 0,
  (1 o 4) o 4 != 1 o (4 o 4) for s = 1).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C08.md#theorem-1-clm-c08-01--every-shifted-knuth-product-is-associative
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C08_Knuth.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C08-01; EXP-C08-03; EXP-C08-05
- **novelty_basis:**

  Knuth 1988; Arnoux 1989; all shifts s >= 2 and the failures at s = 0, 1 are in A. Messaoudi, Math. Slovaca 50 (2000), Thm 6 and Prop 1 (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md). The Lean proof is a verification asset.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C08-03: Recurrences with non-increasing coefficients and canonical initial values give associative circle products for large shifts

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For base sequences G_k = a_1 G_(k-1) + ... + a_d G_(k-d) with a_1 >= a_2 >= ... >= a_d >= 1 and canonical
  initial values (the conditions of Grabner-Petho-Tichy-Woeginger 1994), the circle product o_s is
  associative once the shift s is large enough. (The earlier title, "Pisot base sequences ...", overstated
  the published theorem.)

- **evidence:** EXP-C08-02; EXP-C08-03; EXP-C08-06
- **novelty_basis:**

  Published theorem (P. J. Grabner, A. Petho, R. F. Tichy, G. J. Woeginger, Appl. Math. Lett. 7 (1994) 85-90); see also A. Messaoudi, Math. Slovaca 50 (2000) 135-148 (window criterion, Thm 2-3). Not re-proved by the lab; our data agree for the sequences tested. The sequence G(k+1) = G(k) + G(k-3) (no associative shift <= 20) may be outside the theorem's hypotheses (Historian audit, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).

- **status history:**

### CLM-C08-04: Non-monotone associative-shift sets require a negative conjugate root

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For quadratic Pisot base sequences with real conjugate root beta > 0, the set of shifts s for which o_s is
  associative is upward-closed. Sets that are not upward-closed (e.g. {2} and s >= 4 for G = 1,3,4,7,11,...)
  occur only when beta < 0.

- **evidence:** EXP-C08-04; EXP-C08-03; EXP-C08-06
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low confidence. Not found in Knuth 1988, Arnoux 1989, Grabner et al. 1994, Messaoudi 2000 (read in full) or Ambroz-Frougny-Masakova-Pelantova 2003. The Historian derives from Messaoudi's Thm 2 that for canonical (Parry) sequences the associative shifts are upward-closed whatever the sign of beta; so the phenomenon concerns non-canonical initial values only (reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).

- **limitations:**

  Re-tested with certificates (EXP-C08-06, the same 90 quadratic sequences, shifts 0..10) - 6 rows of EXP-C08-04 listed shifts that are certified non-associative, including two of the original non-monotone examples ((4,3;1,5) at s = 4, (3,3;1,2) at s = 7). After the re-test 10 rows are still non-monotone, all with non-canonical initial values and beta < 0; none of the 30 rows with beta > 0. Isolated 'associative' shifts are supported by search only (no proof). Note that for G(k+1) = pG(k) + qG(k-1) with q > 0 the conjugate root is automatically negative.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C08-05: Measured associativity thresholds for 45 base sequences (refuted - four thresholds were too small)

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  Smallest shift s0 with o_s associative for all tested s0 <= s <= max tested: Fibonacci 2, Pell (1,2,5) 1,
  G(k+1)=2G(k)+2G(k-1) (1,2) 5, tribonacci 3, 4-bonacci 7, G(k+1)=G(k)+G(k-2) 10, G(k+1)=3G(k)+3G(k-1) (1,5) 10;
  G(k+1)=G(k)+G(k-3) none up to 20 (full table in results/EXP-C08-02.json and EXP-C08-03.json).

- **evidence:** EXP-C08-02; EXP-C08-03; EXP-C08-06
- **counterexample:**

  Certified violations (EXP-C08-06) - tribonacci s = 3: (10016 o 10016) o 1 = 3414681926 != 3414681924 = 10016 o (10016 o 1) (the example of Ambroz-Frougny-Masakova-Pelantova 2003); 4-bonacci s = 7: triple (623729, 581581, 1); G(k+1) = 2G(k) + 2G(k-1) (1,2) s = 5: triple (29957, 280628, 1); G(k+1) = G(k) + G(k-2) s = 10: triple (78, 248, 1) (found by the Historian). The random tests behind the measurement (triples up to 10^6) missed these rare failures. Corrected values in CLM-C08-07.

- **novelty_basis:** Partial rediscovery - thresholds known in the literature (Messaoudi 2000, 2002; Ambroz-Frougny-Masakova-Pelantova 2003 refute Arnoux's conjecture that tribonacci s = 3 works).
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:10Z verification: COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED → REFUTED (sync:C08; Historian audit and certified re-test 2026-10-18 - four listed thresholds are wrong)
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:10Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C08; Historian audit and certified re-test 2026-10-18 - four listed thresholds are wrong)

### CLM-C08-06: H-C08-1: Knuth's associative circle product is an isolated case (refuted)

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:** Among digit-convolution products a o_s b = sum d_i d_j G_(i+j+s), only Fibonacci with s = 2 is associative.
- **evidence:** EXP-C08-01; EXP-C08-02
- **counterexample:** Fibonacci with s = 3 (t = 1) is associative by Theorem 1 (CLM-C08-01); geometric bases are associative for every s; many Pisot sequences are associative at larger shifts (EXP-C08-02).
- **novelty_basis:** Grabner-Petho-Tichy-Woeginger 1994 and Messaoudi 2000 exhibit infinitely many associative circle products (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:10Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C08; Historian audit 2026-10-18 (reviews/historian_C03_C10.md))

### CLM-C08-07: Corrected associativity thresholds - certified lower bounds and searched ranges

- **cycle:** C08
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **proof_check:**

  lower bounds certified by explicit triples (results/EXP-C08-06.json); upper bounds proved only where the literature proves them (Fibonacci s >= 2 Lean; Pell s >= 1 Messaoudi 2000 Thm 1; tribonacci s >= 6 per Messaoudi 2002 as reported by Ambroz-Masakova-Pelantova 2007, s >= 9 GPTW; 4-bonacci s >= 10 Messaoudi 2000 Remark 2)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For the circle product o_s: Fibonacci (1,2) fails for s <= 1 and is associative for s >= 2 (Lean, CLM-C08-01);
  Pell (1,2,5) fails at s = 0, no failure found for 1 <= s <= 5 (associative for s >= 1 by Messaoudi 2000);
  G(k+1) = 2G(k) + 2G(k-1) (1,2) fails for s <= 5, no failure found for 6 <= s <= 8; tribonacci (1,2,4) fails
  for s <= 3, no failure found for 4 <= s <= 9; 4-bonacci (1,2,4,8) fails for s <= 7, no failure found for
  8 <= s <= 10; G(k+1) = G(k) + G(k-2) (1,2,3) fails for s <= 10, no failure found for s = 11, 12;
  G(k+1) = 3G(k) + 3G(k-1) (1,5) fails for s <= 9, no failure found for 10 <= s <= 12. Every failure is
  certified by an explicit triple; every "no failure" is computational support only.

- **evidence:** EXP-C08-06
- **novelty_basis:** Partial - the tribonacci and 4-bonacci bounds are in the literature (Messaoudi 2000, 2002; AFMP 2003); the other values are the lab's measurements.
- **limitations:**

  Search - all pairs a <= b < 300, 4000 random pairs below 10^6, literature and Historian examples, c < 300 for an explicit triple; 'no failure found' does not prove associativity. Tribonacci s = 4 and s = 5 are open in the sources read (the Historian derives s >= 5 from AFMP's bound on fractional digits and Messaoudi's Thm 2, unchecked).


### CLM-C09-01: Knuth's circle semigroup embeds in Z[phi]; Fibonacci numbers are its units-images

- **cycle:** C09
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C09.md Theorem 1) relying on CLM-C08-01; formulas checked on 89,401 pairs; awaiting independent skeptic review
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  (N>=1, o) with Knuth's product is a commutative cancellative semigroup without identity. The map
  nu(n) = n phi + n*, n* = floor((n+1)/phi), is an injective homomorphism into (Z[phi], x); a o b = ab + ab* + a*b.
  nu(n) is a unit iff n is a Fibonacci number F_k (k >= 2), and F_j o F_k = F_(j+k).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C09.md#theorem-1-clm-c09-01--structure
- **evidence:** EXP-C09-03
- **novelty_basis:** Arnoux 1989; the product formula is Lunnon's formula in OEIS A101330 (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).
- **status history:**

- **cycle:** C09

### CLM-C09-03: Window obstruction: a fixed proportion 1/phi^2 of all integers are circle-irreducible

- **cycle:** C09
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C09.md Theorem 3; Weyl equidistribution); 0 counterexamples up to 1e6, density 0.381966 observed; awaiting independent skeptic review
- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  If n is a circle product then nu'(n) = n psi + n* lies in (psi^3, psi^2), i.e. frac((n+1)/phi) lies in
  (1/phi - psi^2, 1/phi - psi^3) = (0.2360..., 0.8541...). Hence every n with frac((n+1)/phi) outside this
  interval is circle-irreducible, and these n have natural density exactly 1/phi^2 = 0.381966...

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C09.md#theorem-3-clm-c09-03--the-window-obstruction-and-density-1φ²
- **evidence:** EXP-C09-04
- **novelty_basis:**

  Irreducibles/reducibles of Knuth's product are not in the OEIS (2026-10-18); no study of circle-product factorisation found in the C08 literature search. Elementary consequence of Arnoux's embedding; may exist in the literature on factorisation in submonoids of rings of integers.

### CLM-C10-01: Average cost of counting in classical representations (b/(b-1), e-1, 1)

- **cycle:** C10
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C10.md Theorem 1); confirmed to 6 digits for n < 1e6
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The average number of digit changes per increment is b/(b-1) in base b, 2 in negabinary, 3/2 in balanced
  ternary, e - 1 in the factorial base and exactly 1 in the binary-reflected Gray code.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C10.md#theorem-1-clm-c10-01--classical-constants
- **evidence:** EXP-C10-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  Classical (Cormen et al., ch. 17); see V. Berthe, C. Frougny, M. Rigo, J. Sakarovitch, On the cost and complexity of the successor function, Adv. Appl. Math. 2020 (BFRS) (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).

- **notes:**

  Cost measure here: the number of digit positions that change between the representations of n and n+1 (Hamming distance). BFRS use carry propagation, under which the Zeckendorf value is phi^2 instead of 2.

- **status history:**

### CLM-C10-02: Zeckendorf counting: T_m = 2 G_m - ceil(m/2) - 2, average cost 2

- **cycle:** C10
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-C
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C10.md Theorem 2); recurrence equals brute force for m <= 24
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The total number of digit changes when counting from 0 to G_m - 1 in the Zeckendorf system (G = 1,2,3,5,...)
  is T_m = 2 G_m - ceil(m/2) - 2, satisfying T_(m+1) = T_m + T_(m-1) + ceil(m/2) + 1; the average cost of
  Zeckendorf counting tends to 2.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C10.md#theorem-2-clm-c10-02--zeckendorf-counting
- **evidence:** EXP-C10-02; EXP-C10-04
- **novelty_basis:** In substance - the closed form of T_m is OEIS A215005 (Kotesovec); potential method; see BFRS 2020 for the carry-propagation measure (Historian audit 2026-10-18, reviews/historian_C03_C10.md).
- **notes:** Cost measure: Hamming distance between consecutive representations (not BFRS's carry propagation).
- **status history:**
