### CLM-C16-12: Affine magmas: periods are orders of companion matrices; the anchored magma x + y + 1 is the Fibonacci sequence mod N

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C16.md Theorem 9, conjugation to the linear map and a cyclic vector); Lean for the anchored conjugacy (anchored_fibonacci); exhaustive confirmation for anchored N <= 60, odd N <= 61 and 51 golden pairs (EXP-C16-03)

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For x*y = alpha x + beta y + gamma on Z/N (beta a unit) whose pair map has a fixed point, the maximal period is the
  order of [[0,1],[beta,alpha]] mod N. In particular the anchored associative magma x + y + 1 has exactly the cycle
  structure of the Fibonacci numbers mod N (maximal period = the Pisano period), the odd-law magma x + 1 + y/2 has
  maximal period ord([[0,1],[1/2,1]]) and the golden-law magmas x + 1 + a y (a^2 + a = 1) have maximal period
  ord([[0,1],[a,1]]).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-9-clm-c16-12--affine-magmas
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C16_Dynamics.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  Periods of second-order linear recurrences modulo N are orders of companion matrices (M. Ward, Trans. AMS 35 (1933); D. D. Wall, Amer. Math. Monthly 67 (1960) for Fibonacci). The resulting period sequences of the odd and golden laws (6, 24, 48, 18, 5, ... and 4, 120, 342, ...) are not in the OEIS.


### CLM-C16-13: For affine magmas the maximal period equals the order of the 3x3 affine matrix

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** conjecture
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** For every affine magma x*y = alpha x + beta y + gamma on Z/N with beta a unit, the maximal period of the pair map equals the order of [[0,1,0],[beta,alpha,gamma],[0,0,1]] mod N.
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-03
- **limitations:**

  300 of 300 random cases with N <= 30. Proven only when the pair map has a fixed point (CLM-C16-12) - in general the matrix order is the lcm of all orbit lengths, and equality with the maximum needs a vector whose orbit attains the lcm.


### CLM-C16-14: Fibonacci sequences of nimbers: maximal period pi(2^(2^k) - 1)

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C16.md Theorem 10, discrete logarithms in the field of nimbers); confirmed for q = 16, 256 (EXP-C16-03)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  On [0, q), q = 2^(2^k), with nim-multiplication the pair map sends pairs containing 0 to (0, 0) within two steps and is isomorphic on nonzero pairs to the Fibonacci pair map on (Z/(q-1))^2; its maximal period is pi(q - 1) (40 for q = 16, 360 for q = 256).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-10-clm-c16-14--fibonacci-sequences-of-nimbers
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-03
- **novelty_basis:** The Fibonacci length of a cyclic group is its Pisano period (classical; Knox 1992, Campbell et al.); nimber fields are Conway's.

### CLM-C16-15: Laver tables: every Fibonacci orbit ends in the fixed point or one of the N - 1 two-cycles through the top element

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C16.md Theorem 11) from Laver's facts (N-1)*y = y, x*(N-1) = N-1, x*y > x for x < N-1 (Dehornoy, Braids and Self-Distributivity, Ch. X), which EXP-C16-03 also checks for n <= 10; Lean for the 2-cycle lemma (top_two_cycle); cycle structure confirmed for n <= 10

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  For n >= 1 and N = 2^n, the pair map of the Laver table A_n (0-based) has exactly N cycles - the fixed point
  (N-1, N-1) and the two-cycles {(N-1, c), (c, N-1)} - and every orbit reaches one of them; so the maximal period is 2
  (H-C16-4, 'the maximal period is a power of 2', holds for this degenerate reason).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C16.md#theorem-11-clm-c16-15--laver-tables-collapse-to-2-cycles
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C16_Dynamics.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-03
- **novelty_basis:**

  No source connecting Fibonacci-type recurrences with Laver tables was found (web search 2026-10-18; Laver-table surveys arXiv:1810.00548, Wikipedia). An easy consequence of Laver's monotonicity; small.


### CLM-C16-16: Fibonacci orbits in Laver tables reach the top element within 5 steps (n <= 10)

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** The longest transient of the Laver pair map (steps until the state contains the top element) is 1, 3, 3 for n = 1, 2, 3 and exactly 5 for every n = 4, ..., 10.
- **evidence:** EXP-C16-03
- **limitations:** No explanation; the proof of CLM-C16-15 only gives the bound N.

### CLM-C16-17: Magma Fibonacci generators are not competitive pseudo-random generators

- **cycle:** C16
- **type:** algorithmic
- **system:** SYS-E
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **improvement:** NO IMPROVEMENT
- **statement:**

  On a simple battery (byte chi-square, serial pairs, lowest-bit period, lag-1 correlation; 2^20 outputs) the anchored Fibonacci and affine magma generators on Z/2^32 fail low-byte uniformity and have lowest-bit period 3, and the de Bruijn byte magma (maximal possible period 65536) is too regular; PCG64 passes every test.

- **evidence:** EXP-C16-04
- **novelty_basis:**

  Low-bit periodicity of additive generators modulo 2^w and the non-uniformity of Fibonacci numbers modulo 2^k are classical (Knuth, TAOCP vol. 2, 3.2.2; H. Niederreiter, Fibonacci Quart. 10 (1972) - uniform distribution only modulo powers of 5).

- **limitations:** A sanity battery, not TestU01 or PractRand.

### CLM-C17-01: Light's test: generators suffice for associativity

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C17.md Theorem 1, four-line closure argument); 0 wrong verdicts in EXP-C17-01; awaiting independent skeptic review
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:** If S generates the finite magma M, then M is associative iff (x*s)*y = x*(s*y) for all x, y in M and s in S; the test costs 2n + 2n^2 probes per generator.
- **proof:** proofs/paper/C17.md#theorem-1-clm-c17-01--lights-associativity-test
- **evidence:** EXP-C17-01
- **novelty_basis:** F. W. Light's associativity test, presented in A. H. Clifford, G. B. Preston, The Algebraic Theory of Semigroups, Vol. 1 (AMS, 1961), section 1.2.

### CLM-C17-02: Associativity of an anchored magma is decided by one O(n^2) identity

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C17.md Corollary 2) from C06 Theorem 1 (Lean anchored_assoc_unique); 0 wrong verdicts on 30 anchored instances (EXP-C17-01)
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** If x*0 = x + 1 (mod n), then * is associative iff x*(y + 1) = (x*y) + 1 for all x, y (2n^2 probes); equivalently Light's test with the single generator 0.
- **proof:** proofs/paper/C17.md#corollary-2-clm-c17-02--anchored-magmas-one-generator-and-an-on-criterion
- **evidence:** EXP-C17-01; EXP-C17-02
- **novelty_basis:** Immediate from Light's test and the lab's C06 Theorem 1; small.

### CLM-C17-03: The Rajagopalan-Schulman randomised associativity test is one-sided

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  one-sidedness proved (proofs/paper/C17.md Theorem 3, bilinear extension); the 1/8 detection bound is the cited theorem of Rajagopalan-Schulman, not re-proved; empirically 0 errors in 90 instances with 40 rounds

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The GF(2) subset-sum test (compare (u*v)*w and u*(v*w) in the magma algebra for random subsets u, v, w) never rejects an associative table, and rejects a non-associative one with probability at least 1/8 per round; each round costs O(n^2) probes.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C17.md#theorem-3-clm-c17-03--the-rajagopalanschulman-test-is-one-sided
- **evidence:** EXP-C17-01
- **novelty_basis:** S. Rajagopalan, L. J. Schulman, Verification of identities, SIAM J. Comput. 29 (2000) 1155-1168.

### CLM-C17-04: Benchmark - generator and anchor tests versus the exhaustive check

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** algorithmic
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **improvement:** MIXED
- **statement:**

  On cyclic groups, anchored tables, random magmas and their one-entry corruptions (n = 32..512), Light's test with a greedy generating set uses 23-614 times fewer table probes than the exhaustive n^3 check (generating-set cost included) and the anchored test 2n^2 probes; on left-zero bands the gain is only 1.7x. The randomised test (40 rounds) made no error; random-triple sampling with n^2/16 samples missed 27 of 30 near-associative inputs.

- **evidence:** EXP-C17-01
- **novelty_basis:** The algorithms are classical (Light; Rajagopalan-Schulman); the benchmark is ours. Timings were measured on a shared, loaded machine and are indicative only; probe counts are exact.
- **limitations:** Families chosen to include worst cases for each method; no comparison with optimised C implementations.
- **status history:**

### CLM-C17-05: The anchor instance of left-distributivity singles out the Laver tables (N <= 10)

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  For 2 <= N <= 10, an anchored magma satisfies x*(y+1) = (x*y)*(x+1) for all x, y mod N exactly when N is 2, 4 or 8 and the magma is the Laver table; so for these N, left-distributivity of an anchored table is decided by an O(N^2) check.

- **evidence:** EXP-C17-02
- **novelty_basis:** Probably a consequence of known Laver-table theory (row N is the identity and p*N = N exactly for powers of 2); not checked against the literature.
- **limitations:** Complete SAT enumeration for N <= 10 only.

### CLM-C17-06: H-C17-1: Light's test beats the exhaustive check by a factor >= n/10 on every family (refuted)

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** Light's test with a greedy generating set uses at most a fraction 10/n of the exhaustive check's probes on every input family.
- **evidence:** EXP-C17-01
- **counterexample:**

  Left-zero band x*y = x (n generators needed): ratio 1.7 at n = 512; also one-entry corruptions of cyclic groups (ratio 47.9 < 51.2 at n = 512, because the exhaustive check stops at the first violation).


### CLM-C17-07: H-C17-2: for anchored magmas the z = 0 instance of a law decides the law (refuted)

- **cycle:** C17
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** For every law of the C06 family, every anchored magma satisfying the instance z = 0 of the law satisfies the law (N <= 5).
- **evidence:** EXP-C17-02
- **counterexample:**

  18 laws, e.g. x(yz) = y(y(xz)) (two violating models among 11 at N = 4), x(yz) = x((xy)z), (xy)z = (zy)x, (xy)z = (x(zz))y (EXP-C17-02). It does hold for associativity and (N <= 10) for left-distributivity.


### CLM-C18-01: e-transformations are bijections of strings of fixed length

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** Lean 4 + Mathlib (eT_injective, eT_length; standard axioms only) plus the finite-cardinality step (written proof, proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 1); d(e(X)) = X for all seven quasigroups (EXP-C18-01)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  For every quasigroup Q, leader l and length n, the e-transformation e_l is a bijection of Q^n, inverted by the d-transformation a_i = b_(i-1) \ b_i; hence every composition of forward and reversed e-transformations is a bijection of Q^n.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-1-clm-c18-01--e-transformations-are-bijections
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C18_Quasigroups.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  S. Markovski, D. Gligoroski, V. Bakeva, Quasigroup string processing, Part 1, Contributions, Sec. Math. Tech. Sci., MANU (1999) - the E- and D-transformations with the same leader are mutually inverse permutations of strings (as cited in US patent 8041031, https://image-ppubs.uspto.gov/dirsearch-public/print/downloadPdf/8041031, and in Markovski-Bakeva, Quasigroup String Processing Part 4, https://www.academia.edu/115482463/Quasigroup_String_Processing_Part_4).


### CLM-C18-02: Differences propagate forward only; forward-only tail hashes fail the strict avalanche criterion

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib for the persistence of differences (eT_ne_of_ne; standard axioms only); agreement before i and the corollary by written proof (proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 2); 0 violations on 7 x 2000 random strings (EXP-C18-01); max bias 0.5 for all three forward-only variants (EXP-C18-02)

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  If u and v differ exactly at position i, then e_l(u) and e_l(v) agree at positions < i and differ at every position >= i. Consequently, in a hash that outputs the last 8 bytes of forward e-transformations only, changing a message byte at position i >= n - 8 leaves the earlier tail bytes unchanged, so some output bits never flip and the maximal avalanche bias is 1/2.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-2-clm-c18-02--a-difference-propagates-forward-and-only-forward
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C18_Quasigroups.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-01; EXP-C18-02
- **novelty_basis:** Elementary; implicit in the quasigroup-string-processing literature.

### CLM-C18-03: The tail-information bound: an e-transformation tail carries one byte of state

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Lean 4 + Mathlib for the factorisation of the tail through one state (eT_append, eT_tail_factors) and injectivity (eT_injective); the counting consequences by written proof (proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 3); the bound is attained exactly (256 values) in 100 families of 4096 keys (EXP-C18-05) and explains the 32,624 full collisions of EXP-C18-03 exactly

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  The last k outputs of an e-transformation are a bijective function of the state before them and the last k
  inputs. Hence a hash outputting the last 8 bytes after p forward e-transformations takes at most 256^p values on
  messages sharing their last 7 bytes, and with a final reversed e-transformation it takes at most 256 values on
  messages sharing their first 7 bytes, for every p and every quasigroup of order 256.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-3-clm-c18-03--the-tail-information-bound
- **lean:** proofs/lean/Infinity/C18_Quasigroups.lean
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-03; EXP-C18-05
- **novelty_basis:**

  An elementary narrow-pipe property of byte-level e-transformations. Quasigroup hash designs submitted to the SHA-3 competition used huge quasigroups in wide-pipe constructions (Edon-R, orders 2^256-2^512, https://ehash.isec.tugraz.at/uploads/7/74/Edon.pdf; NaSHA, https://eprint.iacr.org/2008/519), which avoids it; two searches found no explicit statement of the bound for byte-level e-transformation hashes.


### CLM-C18-04: T-function quasigroups (affine mod 256, XOR, (x + 1) XOR y) cannot mix high input bits into low output bits

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 4, induction along the chains using x = x' and y = y' mod 2^r => x*y = x'*y' mod 2^r); 0 low-bit changes for AFF, XOR, ALP in EXP-C18-01; max bias 0.5 in EXP-C18-02

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  If x*y is a T-function on Z/2^8, then in every QG hash (any number of forward and reversed e-transformations) bit j of every output byte depends only on bits 0..j of the message bytes; flipping an input bit at position r never changes an output bit at a position < r, and the maximal avalanche bias is 1/2.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-4-clm-c18-04--the-t-function-obstruction
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-01; EXP-C18-02
- **novelty_basis:**

  Closure of T-functions under composition - A. Klimov, A. Shamir, A new class of invertible mappings, CHES 2002; see https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T-function and https://www.iacr.org/archive/fse2004/30170001/30170001.pdf.


### CLM-C18-05: GF(2)-affine quasigroups give GF(2)-affine hashes with key-independent differences

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 5); one distinct output difference over 2000 keys (EXP-C18-01); mean bias 0.5 for QG-XOR and for CRC32 (EXP-C18-02)
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:** If x*y = A(x) XOR B(y) XOR c with A, B linear over GF(2) (for example x XOR y), every QG hash satisfies H(M XOR D) = H(M) XOR L(D) for a fixed linear L; every avalanche cell is 0 or 1.
- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-5-clm-c18-05--gf2-affine-quasigroups-give-affine-hashes
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-01; EXP-C18-02
- **novelty_basis:** Folklore (affine maps compose to affine maps; the same property makes CRCs unsuitable as hash-table mixers).

### CLM-C18-06: The anchored quasigroup x + c(y): additive differences persist, and the one-pass hash is anagram-blind

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 6); 2000/2000 one-pass hashes unchanged under prefix permutations (EXP-C18-01); bit independence 1.0 with ideal per-bit flip rates for QG-ARP p2+rev (EXP-C18-02); 36,720 full collisions for QG-ARP p1 on near-duplicates (EXP-C18-03)

- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  For x*y = x + c(y) mod 256, two runs over the same inputs from states b, b' differ by the constant b' - b at every position, and one pass computes prefix sums l + sum c(a_i). Hence the one-pass tail hash is unchanged by permutations of the message bytes before the tail, and with a reverse pass all 8 output bytes change by one common additive constant when a message byte at position >= 8 changes (perfectly correlated bit changes).

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-6-clm-c18-06--the-anchored-quasigroup-x--cy-keeps-additive-differences
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-01; EXP-C18-02; EXP-C18-03
- **novelty_basis:** Elementary; shows that an anchored quasigroup of C06 (Theorem 4 there) is a poor mixing primitive.

### CLM-C18-07: The 8-pass cascade has a bijective 64-bit state update

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** written proof (proofs/paper/C18.md Theorem 7, triangular composition of row and column bijections); quality of the cascades measured separately (EXP-C18-05)
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  In the cascade QC(T, d) (d e-transformations run in parallel, output = the final state of every pass), the update of the state (s_1, ..., s_d) by one input byte is a bijection of Q^d; so states are never merged and the tail-information bound does not apply to the output.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C18.md#theorem-7-clm-c18-07--the-cascade-keeps-a-full-8-byte-state
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-05

### CLM-C18-08: H-C18-1: QG-LS with 2 passes and a reverse pass has BLAKE2b-level avalanche

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** QG-LS p2+rev has mean and maximal avalanche bias within 0.01 of BLAKE2b-64 on 2000 random 16-byte keys.
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-02
- **limitations:**

  Holds (0.0089 / 0.041 versus 0.0089 / 0.045, also with one forward pass), but the same hash fails every structured distribution test (CLM-C18-03) - single-bit avalanche statistics cannot see the tail-information bound.


### CLM-C18-09: H-C18-2: structured quasigroups are worse mixers than a random Latin square (maximal avalanche bias)

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** INCONCLUSIVE
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** Every structured quasigroup tested (affine, XOR, x + c(y), (x + 1) XOR y) gives a larger maximal avalanche bias than the random Latin square in the same construction.
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-02
- **limitations:**

  Proved (bias 1/2) for the T-function and GF(2)-affine quasigroups (CLM-C18-04, CLM-C18-05). For x + c(y) with a reverse pass the difference (0.043 versus 0.041) is within sampling noise; its defect shows in bit independence instead (CLM-C18-06), which the hypothesis did not measure.


### CLM-C18-10: H-C18-3: the nimber-field quasigroup gives a hash competitive with FNV-1a (refuted)

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  QG-NIMINV p2+rev (x*y = (x XOR y)^-1 in the nimber field of order 256) has avalanche at least as good as FNV-1a-64 and, like FNV-1a, |chi-square z| <= 3 and a bucket-collision ratio within 2% on all four key sets.

- **evidence:** EXP-C18-02; EXP-C18-03
- **counterexample:**

  Avalanche is better than FNV-1a's (max bias 0.0415 vs 0.5), but on the decimal strings 0..65535 the 1024-bucket z-score is 1084 and the bucket-collision ratio 2.62, and near-duplicate keys give 32,624 full 64-bit collisions (FNV-1a: z = -9.8, ratio 0.995, 0 full collisions) - the tail-information bound (CLM-C18-03).


### CLM-C18-11: H-C18-8: the 8-pass cascade over LS, ISO and NIMINV passes every test (refuted)

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:**

  The cascades QC-LS, QC-ISO and QC-NIMINV (depth 8, 8 zero bytes of finalisation) have avalanche within 0.01 of BLAKE2b-64, |z| <= 3, collision ratios within 2% and no full collisions on all four key sets.

- **evidence:** EXP-C18-05
- **counterexample:**

  QC-NIMINV d8 - maximal avalanche bias 0.0845 against 0.045 for BLAKE2b (about 7 standard deviations of a cell) and z = -3.21 (1021 buckets) on synthetic words; QC-LS d8 and QC-ISO d8 pass every test (EXP-C18-05).


### CLM-C18-12: H-C18-9: the cascade over x + c(y) still fails bit independence (refuted)

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** hypothesis
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** The cascade QC-ARP d8 has a maximal bit-independence correlation of at least 0.5.
- **evidence:** EXP-C18-05
- **counterexample:** QC-ARP d8 has maximal correlation 0.176 (noise level about 0.11) - the cascade removes the perfect correlation of QG-ARP p2+rev, although 5 avalanche cells still exceed bias 0.1 (EXP-C18-05).

### CLM-C18-13: Quasigroup hashes against non-cryptographic baselines - no improvement

- **cycle:** C18
- **type:** algorithmic
- **system:** SYS-B
- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **improvement:** NO IMPROVEMENT
- **statement:**

  Among 13 tail-output quasigroup hashes and 4 cascades, measured against FNV-1a-64, MX64 (multiply-xorshift with the splitmix64 finaliser), BLAKE2b-64 and CRC32 on avalanche, bit independence, chi-square, collisions and throughput, no quasigroup construction improves on the best baseline in any measured dimension.

- **evidence:** EXP-C18-02; EXP-C18-03; EXP-C18-04; EXP-C18-05
- **novelty_basis:**

  Quasigroup string processing for cryptographic primitives and pseudo-random generators is established (Markovski et al.; Gligoroski, Candidate one-way functions and one-way permutations based on quasigroup string transformations, https://ia.cr/2005/352; Edon-R); the benchmark against these baselines is ours.

- **limitations:** Python/numpy implementations on a shared machine (times indicative); a small subset of SMHasher-style tests; one random Latin square (not uniformly sampled); 16-byte keys for avalanche.

### CLM-C20-01: aab -> ababa terminates, with derivational complexity D(n) = 2^(n-2) - 1

- **cycle:** C20
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C20.md Theorem 1 - run encoding, journeys of surplus a's under the rightmost strategy, doubling bound, random descent from the Lean-verified C04 theorem unbordered_random_descent); verified on all words of length <= 12 (EXP-C20-01); awaiting independent skeptic review

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The one-rule system aab -> ababa over {a, b} (and its images under reversal and letter exchange) terminates, and the maximal derivation length from words of length n >= 2 is exactly 2^(n-2) - 1, attained by a^(n-1) b.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C20.md#theorem-1-clm-c20-01--aab--ababa-terminates-and-dn--2n2--1-n--2
- **evidence:** EXP-C20-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  Termination is a rediscovery - the rule is right barren in McNaughton's sense (OVL(r,l) = {a}, r_alpha = abab; OVL(l,r) = {ab}, l_beta = a, never a suffix of (abab)^k), and every non-overlapping left/right barren one-rule system terminates (R. McNaughton, RPI tech. reports 94-18 and 95-15; A. Geser, ICASE Report 2002-33, Thm 3.2/3.3, https://ntrs.nasa.gov/api/citations/20020088729/downloads/20020088729.pdf). The exact complexity 2^(n-2) - 1 was not found in the sources read (the report proves linear lengths only for its non-barren case).


### CLM-C20-02: aab -> babaa terminates; exact per-word derivation length and D(n) = max_b b(2^floor((n-b)/2) - 1)

- **cycle:** C20
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  written proof (proofs/paper/C20.md Theorem 2 - a pair of a's travels to the last run in d steps, doubling d; random descent); per-word formula and D(n) verified on all words of length <= 12 (EXP-C20-01); awaiting independent skeptic review

- **novelty:** POTENTIALLY NOVEL
- **statement:**

  The one-rule system aab -> babaa (and its symmetric images) terminates. For w = a^(x_0) b a^(x_1) b ... b a^(x_m),
  every maximal derivation has length sum_{k=m-1..0} d_k (2^floor(x_k/2) - 1) with d_k = (m - k) + (steps
  contributed by the runs right of k); the derivational complexity is D(n) = max_{1<=b<=n} b(2^floor((n-b)/2) - 1),
  attained by a^(n-b) b^b.

- **proof:** proofs/paper/C20.md#theorem-2-clm-c20-02--aab--babaa-terminates-an-exact-formula-for-dw
- **evidence:** EXP-C20-01
- **novelty_basis:**

  Termination follows from the right-barren criterion (OVL(r,l) = {a, aa} with r_alpha in {baba, bab}; OVL(l,r) = {b}, l_beta = aa, never a suffix) - a rediscovery; the exact per-word formula and the complexity were not found in McNaughton's or Geser's reports (searches 2026-10-18). Small.


### CLM-C20-03: The C04 extended termination census is complete - 480 of the 868 rules with |l| <= 3, |r| <= 5 terminate

- **cycle:** C20
- **type:** theorem
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:** computer-assisted certificates for 860 rules (EXP-C04-02, CLM-C04-01/02 methods) plus Theorems 1-2 of proofs/paper/C20.md and the symmetry argument for the 8 remaining rules
- **novelty:** NOT ASSESSED
- **statement:** Of the 868 one-rule systems l -> r over {a, b} with 1 <= |l| <= 3, |r| <= 5 (EXP-C04-02), exactly 480 terminate and 388 do not.
- **proof:** proofs/paper/C20.md#corollary-3-clm-c20-03--the-c04-census-is-complete
- **evidence:** EXP-C04-02; EXP-C20-01
- **novelty_basis:** A census; one-rule termination for small rules is classical territory (Kurth 1990; McNaughton; Geser).

### CLM-C20-04: C03 Conjecture 4.6 coincides with the Zantema-Geser characterisation

- **cycle:** C20
- **type:** observation
- **system:** SYS-A
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **proof_check:**

  Theorem statement read verbatim by the Historian from the scanned tech. report UU-CS-1994-44 (https://ics-archive.science.uu.nl/research/techreps/repo/CS-1994/1994-44.pdf; the lab's own retrieval attempts at other hosts failed); equivalence by case analysis (s >= 2p or not, r >= 2q or not) and checked on all parameters <= 40

- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **statement:**

  The lab's Conjecture 4.6 (CLM-C03-10) on termination of 0^p 1^q -> 1^r 0^s is equivalent to Zantema-Geser Theorem 1.1 (terminating iff p >= s, or q >= r, or p < s < 2p and q does not divide r, or q < r < 2q and p does not divide s); hence it is a published theorem.

- **proof:** reviews/historian_C03_C10.md#20-the-zantemageser-theorem-verbatim-statement-and-comparison-priority-item
- **evidence:** EXP-C03-01
- **novelty_basis:** Zantema-Geser 1994/2000 (see CLM-C03-10).
- **limitations:** The lab relies on the published proof.
- **status history:**
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:29Z verification: INCONCLUSIVE → PROVEN (sync:C20; Historian audit 2026-10-18 - the full text was found on another host and compared (formerly INCONCLUSIVE))
  - 2026-10-18T17:10:29Z novelty: NOT ASSESSED → REDISCOVERY (sync:C20; Historian audit 2026-10-18 - the full text was found on another host and compared (formerly INCONCLUSIVE))

## Experiments

### EXP-C01-01: Commutation congruence: classes, complete invariant and normal forms

- **cycle:** C01
- **agent:** Mathematical Explorer
- **question:** In System A with the swap relations xy <-> yx, what distinguishes two words that cannot be transformed into each other?
- **hypothesis:**

  Two words are congruent iff, for every symbol s, deleting all other symbols gives the same word s...s (equal symbol projections). The shortlex-completed system {yx -> xy : x < y} is finite and its normal forms are the sorted words.

- **assumptions:** System A axioms A1-A3; alphabet sizes 2-4; words of bounded length (exact, since the relations preserve length)
- **methodology:**

  For every alphabet/length slice, compute the exact congruence classes by union-find over single swap steps; compare the partition with the projection partition; run shortlex Knuth-Bendix completion and compare normal forms with sorted words.

- **falsification:** Any slice where a class contains two words with different projections, or two words with equal projections lie in different classes, or completion fails/normal form differs.
- **summary:** 25 alphabet/length slices checked exactly (up to 4235 words in the largest slices); all checks passed: True; completion of the swap relations is finite: {yx -> xy | x<y}; normal forms = sorted words
- **analysis:** Exact partitions of 25 slices coincide with symbol-count partitions; completion of the swap relations is the finite system {yx -> xy | x < y} whose normal forms are sorted words.
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY

### EXP-C01-02: One-symbol congruences: index and period of finitely generated congruences

- **cycle:** C01
- **agent:** Mathematical Explorer
- **question:** What structures arise when words over a single symbol a are identified by finitely many relations a^m = a^n?
- **hypothesis:**

  The congruence generated by relations a^{m_i} = a^{n_i} (m_i < n_i) is the monogenic congruence with index M = min m_i and period P = gcd(n_i - m_i): exactly M + P classes, a^x ~ a^y iff x = y or (x, y >= M and x = y mod P).

- **assumptions:** System A, alphabet {a}; exponents are meta-level names for unary numerals; relations with 1-3 equations and exponents <= 20
- **methodology:**

  Two independent methods per relation set: (1) shortlex Knuth-Bendix completion, reading index/period from the completed single rule and counting irreducible words; (2) union-find over exponents 0..B with all shifted relations (B = 6 max n). Compare both with the formula.

- **falsification:** Any relation set where completion yields a different rule, the class count differs from M + P, or the bounded union-find partition of small exponents differs from the formula.
- **summary:**
  - relation sets tested: {'one': 78, 'two': 3081, 'three': 3000} (exhaustive for 1-2 relations with exponents <= 12; 3000 random triples with exponents <= 20)
  - failures of the (min, gcd) formula: 0
  - example a^2=a^5, a^3=a^7 completes to [('aaa', 'aa')] (index 2, period 1)
- **analysis:** All 6159 relation sets agree with index = min m_i and period = gcd(n_i - m_i) under two independent methods.
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **related:** CLM-C01-02; CLM-C01-03
- **runs:** 1; last 2026-10-18T06:30:39Z (1.022 s), results sha256 `3f8f41b942591cd7…`, code `experiments/C01/E02_one_letter_congruences.py`

### EXP-C01-03: Endomorphisms of the one-symbol free monoid form the semiring of natural numbers

- **cycle:** C01
- **agent:** Mathematical Inventor
- **question:** Is there a second operation on unary numerals that arises from transformations alone?
- **hypothesis:**

  Endomorphisms of {a}* (substitutions a -> w) with pointwise juxtaposition (f+g)(u) = f(u)g(u) and composition form a commutative semiring isomorphic to (N, +, x) via f -> f(a). With two symbols modulo commutation, composition is no longer commutative.

- **assumptions:** System A over {a} and over {a,b} modulo commutation; finite window of images
- **methodology:**

  Represent an endomorphism by the image of a (a word). Implement substitution and juxtaposition on strings only; check every semiring axiom on all images a^0..a^K; check the length map is a homomorphism. For {a,b}/commutation, sample substitutions and look for non-commuting compositions.

- **falsification:** Any failure of a semiring axiom, or commuting compositions for all two-symbol samples.
- **summary:** semiring axioms + length homomorphism checked on all images a^0..a^12: 0 failures; two symbols mod commutation: 154/200 random pairs of substitutions do not commute (composition = 2x2 matrix product)
- **analysis:** Substitutions on one symbol form (N, +, x); on two symbols modulo commutation composition is non-commutative (154/200 random pairs).
- **related:** CLM-C01-04
- **runs:** 1; last 2026-10-18T06:30:39Z (0.021 s), results sha256 `a79b511734ae7e73…`, code `experiments/C01/E03_endomorphisms.py`

### EXP-C01-04: Single Parikh-preserving relations versus the Parikh (counting) equivalence

- **cycle:** C01
- **agent:** Mathematical Inventor
- **question:** If a relation u <-> v only rearranges symbols, does it eventually identify all words with the same symbol counts (on long enough words)?
- **hypothesis:**

  H-C01-4 (inventor's conjecture, deliberately bold): for every relation u <-> v over {a,b} with u != v and equal symbol counts, the generated congruence coincides with Parikh equivalence on all words of length >= |u|.

- **assumptions:** System A over {a,b}; relations of length 3 and 4; words of length <= 11
- **methodology:** For each relation (up to swapping sides) compute exact congruence classes of each length (length-preserving, so exact), and compare their number and content with Parikh classes.
- **falsification:** A word of length >= |u| whose class is strictly smaller than its Parikh class.
- **summary:**
  - 33 Parikh-preserving relations of length 3-4 tested on words up to length 11
  - 33 of them give a word whose class is smaller than its Parikh class (e.g. ['aab', 'aba']: {'word': 'aab', 'class': ['aab', 'aba'], 'parikh_class_size': 3})
- **analysis:**

  Every one of the 33 rearranging relations of length 3-4 has words whose class is strictly smaller than their counting class; H-C01-4 refuted. Unexpected regularity in the class counts (Fibonacci and tribonacci partial sums) triggered H-C01-5.

- **counterexamples:** aab <-> aba: class of baa is {baa}, counting class {aab, aba, baa}
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **related:** CLM-C01-05; CLM-C01-06
- **runs:** 1; last 2026-10-18T06:30:39Z (0.149 s), results sha256 `0478849346fbd6ce…`, code `experiments/C01/E04_parikh_preserving_relations.py`

### EXP-C01-05: Class counts modulo one relation u <-> v: the unbordered-side theorem and its exceptions

- **cycle:** C01
- **agent:** Mathematical Explorer
- **question:** Why do all length-3 relations give F(n+3)-1 classes and most length-4 relations give partial sums of tribonacci numbers? Which relations are exceptions?
- **hypothesis:**

  H-C01-5: if |u| = |v| = k, u != v and u is unbordered (no proper prefix equals a suffix), then the number of classes of length-n words over a q-letter alphabet equals [x^n] 1/(1 - q x + x^k), i.e. the number of length-n words avoiding u.

- **assumptions:** System A; alphabets of size 2 (k = 2..5, n <= 12) and 3 (k = 2..3, n <= 7); exact classes (relations preserve length)
- **methodology:**

  Exhaustive over all unordered pairs {u, v} of distinct words of length k. Exact class counts by union-find; compare with the series coefficients; for relations whose sides are both bordered, record counts, the minimal linear recurrence fitted to them, and whether shortlex completion terminates.

- **falsification:** Any relation with an unbordered side whose class counts differ from the series.
- **summary:** 1037 relations tested; 756 have an unbordered side; failures of H-C01-5: 0; 281 relations have both sides bordered; 81 of them deviate from the generic count 1/(1-qx+x^k)
- **analysis:** H-C01-5 holds for all 756 relations with an unbordered side; 81 of the 281 relations with both sides bordered deviate from the generic count, all sharing a common border.
- **related:** CLM-C01-06; CLM-C01-08
- **runs:** 1; last 2026-10-18T06:33:27Z (12.306 s), results sha256 `305a422a5ddd2df1…`, code `experiments/C01/E05_unbordered_relations.py`

- **cycle:** C01

- **question:** Is the number of classes of length-n words modulo u <-> v determined by the common borders of u and v?

  Exact class counts by union-find. 'Hard' pairs are those where neither border set contains the other (the case not covered by the proved containment theorem); for k <= 5 binary and k <= 3 ternary all pairs are tested.

- **falsification:** Any relation whose exact class counts differ from the formula.

### EXP-C01-07: Automatic proofs of the common-border class-count formula via finite completion

- **cycle:** C01
- **agent:** Formal Verifier
- **question:** For which relations u <-> v can H-C01-7 be proved for ALL lengths n by a finite certificate?
- **hypothesis:**

  For most small relations some shortlex order (alphabet permutation, read left-to-right or right-to-left) yields a finite convergent completion; its irreducible words then give a rational count sequence that can be compared with the formula exactly.

- **assumptions:** Newman's lemma and the critical-pair lemma for string rewriting; Cayley-Hamilton bound on the order of DFA count recurrences
- **methodology:**

  For each relation and each of 2*q! shortlex orders: run bounded Knuth-Bendix; on success, independently re-verify convergence (rules decreasing, all critical pairs joinable, u and v have equal normal forms); check soundness directly (every completed rule l -> r is derived from u <-> v by breadth-first search in the class of l); build the Aho-Corasick DFA of the left-hand sides (s live states); compare s + k + 5 count terms with the formula (sufficient for equality of all terms); cross-check against brute-force class counts for n <= 8; store every certificate.

- **falsification:** A verified convergent completion whose irreducible-word counts differ from the formula would refute H-C01-7 for that relation.
- **summary:**
  - 2159 relations attempted; 1632 proved for all n by verified finite completion; 527 without a finite completion (formula still only checked for n <= 12/7); refutations: 0; internal inconsistencies: 0
  - per case: {'ab/k=2': {'containment PROVEN': 5, 'hard PROVEN': 1}, 'ab/k=3': {'containment PROVEN': 24, 'hard PROVEN': 3, 'hard NO FINITE COMPLETION FOUND': 1}, 'ab/k=4': {'containment PROVEN': 87, 'hard PROVEN': 15, 'hard NO FINITE COMPLETION FOUND': 18}, 'ab/k=5': {'containment PROVEN': 360, 'hard PROVEN': 74, 'hard NO FINITE COMPLETION FOUND': 62}, 'ab/k=6/hard-only': {'hard PROVEN': 331, 'hard…
  - 1632 certificates stored; soundness of completed rules (BFS derivation from u = v): {'ok': 1632}
- **analysis:** 1632 relations proved for all n by independently re-verified finite completions; 527 hard relations have infinite completions within the search bounds.
- **related:** CLM-C01-07; CLM-C01-08
- **future:** Parametric (regular) completions for the 527 open relations

### EXP-C02-03: Carry-rule survey: termination criterion, glider certificates and emergent bases

- **cycle:** C02
- **agent:** Mathematical Explorer
- **question:** Which carry rules turn unary numerals into finite normal forms, what is the emergent base, and are the resulting representations ordered like ordinary numerals?
- **hypothesis:**

  H-C02-1: the rule terminates from every unary start iff sum_j b_j < m (emergent base r > 1, r the positive root of sum_j b_j r^j = m); otherwise some start produces a periodic glider. H-C02-2: for terminating rules, n < n' implies rep(n) < rep(n') in radix order.

- **assumptions:** m in {2,3,4}, b_j in {0..3}, J <= 3 (last coefficient nonzero): 189 rules; unary starts n <= 200; representations for n <= 3000
- **methodology:**

  Batch-firing upward sweep. After level k is processed the future depends only on the window (c_{k+1}, ..., c_{k+J}), and the window map is monotone; a window W with an entry >= m that is later dominated componentwise (periodic glider if equal) certifies infinitely many firings. Radix order = compare length, then digits from the top.

- **falsification:** A rule with sum b < m and an infinite derivation, a rule with sum b >= m terminating from all n <= 200 with no glider, or a radix-order inversion.
- **summary:** 189 carry rules; 31 terminate from all unary n <= 200, all with sum b < m; H-C02-1 violations: 0; every non-terminating rule has a certificate (periodic glider or monotone pumping): True; H-C02-2 (radix monotonicity) violations among terminating rules: 0
- **analysis:**

  Termination from unary starts holds exactly for the 31 rules with sum b < m; every one of the 158 other rules has a periodic-glider or monotone-pumping certificate; no radix-order inversion up to n = 3000.

- **related:** CLM-C02-03; CLM-C02-04; CLM-C02-05; CLM-C02-08
- **runs:** 3; last 2026-10-18T08:36:48Z (2.286 s), results sha256 `0fb4267c2593ecea…`, code `experiments/C02/E03_carry_rule_survey.py`

### EXP-C02-04: Uniqueness of stable representations in emergent bases; addition by merging

- **cycle:** C02
- **agent:** Mathematical Explorer
- **question:** In a terminating carry system, can an integer have a stable representation other than the normal form of its unary numeral? Does merging two normal forms and stabilising add them?
- **hypothesis:**

  H-C02-4: for every terminating carry rule (sum b < m), distinct stable configurations of length <= L have distinct values, so each integer has exactly one stable representation. H-C02-5: stab(rep(a) + rep(b)) = rep(a + b).

- **assumptions:** the 31 terminating rules of EXP-C02-03; exact arithmetic in Q(r) modulo the minimal polynomial of r; all stable configurations with at most L levels (m^L <= 2e5)
- **methodology:** Enumerate every stable configuration up to L levels, reduce sum c_k x^k modulo the minimal polynomial of r, keep those whose value is an integer, group by value. Addition: 2000 random pairs per rule.
- **falsification:** Two stable configurations with the same integer value; a merged sum that stabilises to something other than rep(a+b).

- **analysis:** H-C02-4 refuted - four terminating rules have integers with several stable representations; merge-and-stabilise addition always agrees with rep(a+b).

- **cycle:** C02

### EXP-C02-07: Critical rules: non-termination from all large unary starts and the spectral gap

- **cycle:** C02
- **agent:** Formal Verifier
- **question:** Is non-termination of critical rules (sum b = m) a property of all large starts, as a spectral argument predicts?
- **hypothesis:**

  T-C02-12: if sum b = m and gcd{j : b_j > 0} = 1, the window map's linearisation has the simple eigenvalue 1 (eigenvector T = tail sums) and all other eigenvalues of modulus < 1; consequently every unary start n >= n0 reaches a window dominating T and never terminates.

- **assumptions:** all critical rules with m <= 5, J <= 4, b_j <= 4 and gcd of support 1 (b_J > 0); starts n = 1..2000
- **methodology:**

  For each rule: eigenvalues of the J x J linear map (A x)_i = x_(i+1) + (b_i/m) x_1; sweep every start up to 2000 with periodic/pumping certificates; record the largest terminating start n0 - 1 and check that every larger start has a certificate.

- **falsification:** A rule with an eigenvalue of modulus >= 1 other than the simple eigenvalue 1, or a terminating start after the first certified one beyond a stable tail.
- **summary:** 98 critical rules with gcd-1 support (m <= 5, J <= 4): spectral violations 0; every start beyond the largest terminating one is certified non-terminating: True (largest terminating start over all rules: 124); max second-eigenvalue modulus: 0.949051
- **analysis:**

  All 98 critical rules (m <= 5, J <= 4, b_j <= 4, gcd-1 support) have a simple eigenvalue 1 and all other eigenvalues of modulus < 1 (max 0.949); every start beyond the largest terminating one (<= 124) is certified non-terminating - as Theorem 9 predicts.

### EXP-C03-01: Termination survey of the 256 rules 0^p 1^q -> 1^r 0^s (p, q, r, s <= 4)

- **cycle:** C03
- **agent:** Mathematical Explorer
- **question:** Which rules 0^p 1^q -> 1^r 0^s terminate, and what separates terminating from looping rules?
- **hypothesis:**

  Three competing hypotheses are tested. H-C03-1 (naive): terminates iff s <= p or r <= q (some letter count never grows). H-C03-2 (director's first guess): terminates iff r < 2q or s < 2p. H-C03-3 (after the first loops): non-terminating iff s > p, r > q and (r >= 2q or s >= 2p).

- **assumptions:** System A over {0,1}; one rule; loop search = forward closures with right and left extensions, words <= 40 letters, <= 40000 closure pairs; matrix interpretations over N: d = 2 (entries 0..4) and upper-triangular d = 3 (0..3), d = 4 (0..1)
- **methodology:**

  For each rule: (1) proven regions by counting (s < p or r < q) or conservation (s = p or r = q); (2) forward-closure loop search, every certificate re-checked step by step; (3) if no loop, exhaustive matrix-interpretation search, certificate re-checked in exact arithmetic; (4) otherwise bounded evidence: reachable sets from all words of length <= 12 containing the left-hand side.

- **falsification:** A loop certificate for a rule predicted terminating, or a termination certificate for a rule predicted non-terminating.
- **summary:** 256 rules: 220 terminating (certified), 28 non-terminating (loop certificates), 8 unknown; contradictions: H-C03-1 0, H-C03-2 12, H-C03-3 0; unknown rules: [[1, 3, 4, 3], [1, 3, 4, 4], [2, 2, 3, 3], [2, 3, 4, 3], [3, 1, 3, 4], [3, 1, 4, 4], [3, 2, 3, 4], [3, 3, 4, 4]]; reversal-complement symmetry violations: 0
- **analysis:**

  Of 256 rules with parameters <= 4, 220 are certified terminating (218 by matrix interpretations, 2 by the conservation theorem), 28 carry verified loops, 8 remain unknown. H-C03-2 is contradicted by 12 loops; H-C03-1 and H-C03-3 are not contradicted by any certified status.

- **counterexamples:** 0 1 1 -> 1 1 1 0 0 loops: 001111 ->4 111 001111 00100 (contradicts H-C03-2)
- **related:** CLM-C03-01; CLM-C03-02; CLM-C03-06; CLM-C03-08
- **runs:** 1; last 2026-10-18T07:33:53Z (34.391 s), results sha256 `6d2d95a20fd4742d…`, code `experiments/C03/E01_termination_survey.py`

### EXP-C03-02: Extended termination survey of 0^p 1^q -> 1^r 0^s for p, q, r, s <= 5

- **cycle:** C03
- **agent:** Mathematical Skeptic
- **question:** Does H-C03-3 survive a larger family and a stronger loop finder, and are any growing rules provably terminating by matrix interpretations?
- **hypothesis:** H-C03-3: the rule is non-terminating iff r > q, s > p and (r >= 2q or s >= 2p); equivalently it terminates iff r <= q or s <= p or (r < 2q and s < 2p).
- **assumptions:**
  - count-decreasing (s < p or r < q) and conserving (s = p or r = q) rules are terminating by the theorems of proofs/paper/C03.md and are not re-searched
  - both-growing rules: forward closures (<= 40 letters, <= 40000 pairs), then leftmost/rightmost strategies from 0^a 1^b (a <= 3p, b <= max(4q^2, 3q), <= 3000 steps, <= 6000 letters), then matrix interpretations (d = 2 entries 0..4; upper-triangular d = 3 entries 0..3), then bounded evidence from all words of length <= 12
- **methodology:** As in EXP-C03-01 with the additional strategy-based loop finder; every certificate is re-checked independently.
- **falsification:** A loop for a rule with r < 2q and s < 2p (or with r <= q or s <= p), or a matrix termination proof for a rule predicted non-terminating.
- **summary:**
  - 625 rules: 525 terminating (proved or certified), 71 non-terminating (loop certificates: 56 by forward closures, 15 by strategies), 29 unknown
  - contradictions: H-C03-1 0, H-C03-2 35, H-C03-3 0; H-C03-3 predicts ['NON-TERMINATING', 'TERMINATING'] for all unknown rules
  - matrix-certified growing rules: 0; symmetry violations: 10
- **analysis:**

  Extended survey (625 rules, parameters <= 5) with a strategy-based loop finder - 525 terminating by theorem, 71 loops, 29 unknown; H-C03-2 has 35 contradictions; no both-growing rule admits a matrix interpretation in the searched tiers.

- **related:** CLM-C03-06; CLM-C03-08; CLM-C03-09

### EXP-C03-03: Divisibility: loops lift along 0 -> 0^p, non-multiples look terminating

- **cycle:** C03
- **agent:** Mathematical Explorer
- **question:** Why do (2,2,3,4) and (2,2,3,6) loop while (2,2,3,5) and (2,2,3,7) apparently terminate?
- **hypothesis:**

  H-C03-4: 0^p 1^q -> 1^r 0^s is non-terminating iff (a) r >= 2q and s >= 2p, or (b) s >= 2p, q < r < 2q and p | s, or (c) r >= 2q, p < s < 2p and q | r; otherwise it terminates. Mechanism for (b): every rule (1, q, r, k) with q < r < 2q, k >= 2 loops, and the morphism 0 -> 0^p maps it onto (p, q, r, kp).

- **assumptions:**
  - loop certificates re-checked step by step
  - 'terminating from u' is certified by a finite reachable set (finitely branching rewriting: finite reachable set + no cycle = no infinite derivation from u)
- **methodology:**

  Part A: loops for the base family (1, q, r, k), q <= 5, q < r < 2q, k <= 6. Part B: lift every base loop to (p, q, r, kp), p <= 4, and verify. Part C: for p in {2,3}, q in {2,3,4}, q < r < 2q, 2p <= s <= 3p+2: loop search, reachable sets from every 0^a 1^b (a <= 3p, b <= 3q+2) and from every word of length <= 12 containing the left-hand side (reachable words up to 720 letters). Part D: re-examine the 29 unknown rules of EXP-C03-02 using mirrors and lifts. (Reachable-set probes are run for the non-divisible rules only; for divisible rules the loop certificate is the evidence.)

- **falsification:** A base rule (1, q, r, k) without a loop although infinite growth is observed; a non-multiple rule with a loop or an infinite reachable set; a multiple without a loop.
- **summary:**
  - base family (1,q,r,k), q<=5, q<r<2q, k<=6: 49 loops found, missing: [[1, 5, 6, 6]]
  - lifted loops verified (p=2..4, both orientations): 147, failures: 0
  - part C: 66 rules; divisibility predicts loop-finding exactly: True (mismatches []); all non-multiples terminate from every tested start: True
  - EXP-C03-02 unknowns resolved as looping: 5; remaining 24: [[2, 2, 3, 3], [2, 2, 3, 5], [2, 2, 5, 3], [2, 3, 4, 3], [2, 3, 4, 5], [2, 3, 5, 3], [2, 3, 5, 5], [2, 4, 5, 3], [2, 4, 5, 5], [3, 2, 3, 4], [3, 2, 3, 5], [3, 2, 5, 4], [3, 2, 5, 5], [3, 3, 4, 4], [3, 3, 4, 5], [3, 3, 5, 4], [3, 3, 5, 5], [3, 4, 5, 4], [3, 4, 5, 5], [4, 2, 3, 5], [4, 2, 5, 5], [4, 3, 4, 5], [4, 3, 5, 5], [4, 4, 5, 5]]
  - H-C03-4 contradictions on the 625 rules: 0; prediction for remaining unknowns: ['TERMINATING']
- **analysis:**

  Loops are found exactly when p divides s on 66 test rules; all 36 non-multiples terminate from every tested start; 147 lifted loops verify; H-C03-4 has no contradiction on the 625 rules and predicts termination for all 24 remaining unknowns. The first run reported a spurious non-finite word because of a too-tight length cap (96 letters); the reachable set from 001001001111 under 0011 -> 11100000 is finite (232 words, longest 104 letters).

- **related:** CLM-C03-05; CLM-C03-09; CLM-C03-10
- **runs:** 3; last 2026-10-18T09:18:59Z (15.513 s), results sha256 `1a84189b39cd83c4…`, code `experiments/C03/E03_divisibility.py`

- **cycle:** C03
- **agent:** Formal Verifier
- **question:** Does every base rule 0 1^q -> 1^r 0^k with q < r < 2q, k >= 2 loop (as required by the non-terminating half of H-C03-4), and how large are the loops?
- **hypothesis:** All base rules with q <= 6, k <= 6 have loop certificates; loop sizes grow rapidly with q.

- **methodology:** For each base rule: forward closures (<= 60 letters), else long strategy runs; verify each certificate and its lifts along 0 -> 0^p (p = 2..4).

- **analysis:** Every base rule 0 1^q -> 1^r 0^k with q <= 6, q < r < 2q, k <= 6 has a verified loop (75 rules); 225 lifts verify. Loop lengths grow roughly like k^q (largest 9332 steps).

### EXP-C04-01: Termination census of the 196 one-rule string rewriting systems with |l|, |r| <= 3

- **cycle:** C04
- **agent:** Formal Verifier
- **question:** Which one-rule systems over {a,b} with sides of length <= 3 terminate, and can every verdict be backed by a checkable certificate?
- **hypothesis:**

  Every one of the 196 rules is decided by a simple certificate: length decrease, lexicographic monotonicity (length-preserving), a strictly decreasing linear interpretation over N, a decreasing factor count, or a self-embedding derivation w ->+ x w y.

- **assumptions:** System A over {a,b}; 1 <= |l| <= 3, 0 <= |r| <= 3, l != r
- **methodology:** Apply the certificate searches in order; re-check every self-embedding certificate step by step with an independent checker.
- **falsification:** A rule left UNKNOWN, or a certificate that fails the independent check.
- **summary:**
  - 196 rules: {'TERMINATING': 162, 'NON-TERMINATING': 34}; certificates by kind: {'length decreases': 70, 'length-preserving, lexicographically monotone': 70, 'self-embedding': 34, 'linear interpretation': 20, 'factor count decreases': 2}
  - unknown: 0; certificates failing the independent check: 0
  - 22 length-increasing rules terminate (linear interpretation or factor count)
- **analysis:** All 196 one-rule systems with |l|, |r| <= 3 are decided by checkable certificates; the 34 non-terminating ones are exactly those whose left-hand side is a factor of the right-hand side.
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **related:** CLM-C04-01
- **runs:** 1; last 2026-10-18T07:32:35Z (0.003 s), results sha256 `1bc8184c4a5f40dc…`, code `experiments/C04/E01_termination_census.py`

### EXP-C04-02: Extended termination census (|l| <= 3, |r| <= 5) and the factor hypothesis

- **cycle:** C04
- **agent:** Mathematical Inventor
- **question:** In EXP-C04-01 every non-terminating rule had l as a factor of r. Is that a law?
- **hypothesis:** H-C04-0 (bold): a one-rule system l -> r with |l| <= 3 terminates iff l is not a factor of r.
- **assumptions:** System A over {a,b}; 1 <= |l| <= 3, 0 <= |r| <= 5, l != r: 868 rules
- **methodology:**

  Certificate cascade of EXP-C04-01, then linear interpretations with coefficients <= 7, integer weighted factor counts (|f| <= 4) found by LP and re-verified exactly, and a deeper self-embedding search (seeds up to length 10). Undecided rules get bounded evidence.

- **falsification:** A non-terminating rule whose lhs is not a factor of its rhs refutes H-C04-0.
- **summary:** 868 rules: {'TERMINATING': 472, 'NON-TERMINATING': 388, 'UNKNOWN': 8}; H-C04-0 counterexamples (non-terminating, l not a factor of r): 18, e.g. ['ab', 'bbaa'] via ['aab', 'abbaa', 'bbaabaa']; undecided rules: 8 ['aab->ababa', 'aab->babaa', 'abb->babab', 'abb->bbaba', 'baa->aabab', 'baa->ababa', 'bba->ababb', 'bba->babab']
- **analysis:**

  Extending to |r| <= 5 refutes the bold factor hypothesis H-C04-0 (18 non-trivial looping rules, e.g. ab -> bbaa); 860 of 868 rules decided by certificates (linear interpretations with coefficients <= 7 and LP-found, exactly re-verified weighted factor counts added); 8 rules in two symmetry classes remain open.

- **counterexamples:** ab -> bbaa: aab -> abbaa -> bbaabaa contains aab (self-embedding)
- **verification:** REFUTED
- **related:** CLM-C04-02; CLM-C04-03
- **runs:** 1; last 2026-10-18T07:34:52Z (11.724 s), results sha256 `94726db18a386c7e…`, code `experiments/C04/E02_extended_census.py`

### EXP-C04-03: Derivational complexity of terminating one-rule systems (|l|, |r| <= 3)

- **cycle:** C04
- **agent:** Mathematical Explorer
- **question:** How long can derivations of a terminating one-rule system be, as a function of the length of the start word, and which growth classes occur?
- **hypothesis:**

  H-C04-G: every terminating rule in the family has derivational complexity D(n) = max over words of length n of the longest derivation that is linear, quadratic or exponential, and D(n) is given by a linear recurrence (rational generating function).

- **assumptions:** the 162 rules proved terminating in EXP-C04-01; exhaustive derivation graphs from all words of length <= 12 (<= 8 for four exponential rules, extended to 20 by a verified formula)
- **methodology:**

  Exact min/max derivation lengths per word from the acyclic derivation graph; D_max(n), D_min(n); minimal linear recurrence (Berlekamp-Massey, over-determined) and the moduli/multiplicities of its characteristic roots give the growth class. Rules are grouped by the symmetries reversal and letter exchange.

- **falsification:** A rule whose D_max admits no over-determined recurrence, or a growth class other than linear/quadratic/exponential.
- **summary:**
  - 162 terminating rules in 55 symmetry classes; growth classes (rules): {'linear': 140, 'quadratic': 14, 'exponential': 4, 'no recurrence found': 4}
  - doubling-rule formula verified against exhaustive graphs on 2044 words
  - symmetry classes without a floor((a n^2+b n+c)/d) or 2^(n-1)-1 closed form: ['aab->ba']
  - ab->ba D_max: [0, 0, 1, 2, 4, 6, 9, 12, 16, 20, 25, 30, 36]; ab->bba D_max: [0, 0, 1, 3, 7, 15, 31, 63, 127, 255, 511, 1023, 2047, 4095]
- **analysis:**

  Derivational complexity of the 162 terminating rules - 140 linear, 14 quadratic, 4 exponential, 4 non-rational (the aab -> ba class); simple floor-quadratic or 2^(n-1)-1 closed forms for 54 of 55 symmetry classes. Among length-preserving rules with |l| >= 2 only ab <-> ba attains floor(n^2/4), consistent with Bertrand's theorem.

- **related:** CLM-C04-11; CLM-C04-12; CLM-C04-04
- **runs:** 3; last 2026-10-18T07:55:27Z (163.601 s), results sha256 `5039ce5f4ad2cc7e…`, code `experiments/C04/E03_derivational_complexity.py`

### EXP-C04-04: The rule aab -> ba is a truncated binary exchange system: D(n) = max_m (n-m) - ceil((n-m)/2^m)

- **cycle:** C04
- **agent:** Mathematical Explorer
- **question:** Why does the derivational complexity of aab -> ba admit no linear recurrence?
- **hypothesis:**

  H-C04-L: writing w = a^x0 b a^x1 ... b a^xm, each step is the carry 2[k] -> [k+1] (k < m) with level m a sink; hence D(n) = max over m of (n - m) - ceil((n - m)/2^m), i.e. n - D(n) = min_m (m + ceil((n-m)/2^m)) = log2(n) + O(1). Predicted first terms where D stalls: n = 4, 7, 12, 21, 38, ...

- **assumptions:** random descent of aab -> ba (unbordered left-hand side, CLM-C04-05); exhaustive derivation graphs up to n = 17 (EXP-C04-03)
- **methodology:**

  (1) Exhaustive derivation graphs for n <= 14 (independent of the reduction). (2) For n <= 21, one leftmost derivation per word (valid by random descent) over all 2^n words. (3) Per-word check of the configuration formula D(w) = T - s2(V mod 2^m) - floor(V/2^m).

- **falsification:** Any n <= 21 where the maximum over words differs from the formula, or any word whose derivation length differs from the configuration formula.
- **summary:** max derivation length over all 2^n words, n <= 21: [0, 0, 0, 1, 1, 2, 3, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, 15]; formula agrees for all n <= 21: True; per-word configuration formula mismatches: graph (n <= 14) 0, simulation (n <= 21) 0; n where D(n) = D(n-1) (n >= 3, n < 60): [4, 7, 12, 21, 38]; closed form D(n) = n - 1 - min{k : 2^k + k >= n} (n <= 20000): True; stalls exactly at n = 2^k + k + 1: True
- **analysis:**

  The configuration (binary-counter-with-sink) formula for aab -> ba matches every word up to length 21 and the exhaustive graphs; D(n) = n - 1 - min{k : 2^k + k >= n} up to n = 20000; stalls exactly at n = 2^k + k + 1.

- **verification:** PROVEN
- **related:** CLM-C04-09
- **runs:** 3; last 2026-10-18T09:19:15Z (15.951 s), results sha256 `be51ed9422ae3039…`, code `experiments/C04/E04_aab_ba_formula.py`

### EXP-C04-05: Random descent of one-rule systems: unbordered left-hand sides versus critical peaks

- **cycle:** C04
- **agent:** Mathematical Inventor
- **question:** When do all maximal derivations from every word have the same length (random descent)?
- **hypothesis:**

  H-C04-1 (bold): a terminating one-rule system has random descent iff its left-hand side is unbordered. H-C04-2: a terminating one-rule system has random descent iff each of its critical peaks (r s <- p b s -> p r for l = p b = b s) is joinable by two derivations of EQUAL length.

- **assumptions:** the 162 terminating rules of EXP-C04-01; all words of length <= 11 (<= 8 for the four doubling rules)
- **methodology:**

  Exact min/max derivation lengths and the set of reachable normal forms per word from the derivation graph; random descent = all maximal derivations from each word have the same length AND the same normal form (van Oostrom); critical peaks joined by breadth-first search over exact step counts.

- **falsification:** H-C04-1: a bordered rule with random descent or an unbordered one without. H-C04-2: disagreement between observed random descent and the critical-peak test.
- **summary:** 162 terminating rules; (bordered, RD, confluent) counts: {'False/True/True': 84, 'True/False/False': 60, 'True/True/True': 18}; H-C04-1 counterexamples: 18 (e.g. [['aa', ''], ['aa', 'a'], ['aa', 'aba'], ['bb', '']]); unbordered rules without RD: 0; H-C04-2 disagreements (RD vs equal-length joinable critical peaks): 0; length-uniform but not confluent: 0
- **analysis:**

  All 84 unbordered rules have random descent; 18 of 78 bordered rules also do (H-C04-1 refuted); random descent agrees with equal-length joinability of critical peaks in all 162 cases; no rule is length-uniform without being confluent in this family.

- **counterexamples:** aa -> a: bordered left-hand side but random descent
- **related:** CLM-C04-05; CLM-C04-07; CLM-C04-06
- **runs:** 2; last 2026-10-18T08:00:11Z (51.462 s), results sha256 `aaaaa7a552736929…`, code `experiments/C04/E05_random_descent.py`

### EXP-C04-06: Constant-step potentials for one-rule systems; confluence versus random descent

- **cycle:** C04
- **agent:** Formal Verifier
- **question:** Why did no terminating one-rule system turn out confluent without random descent?
- **hypothesis:**

  H-C04-3: for every rule l -> r (l != r) there are a letter c, an exponent K and delta != 0 such that Phi(w) = sum over positions i with w_i = c of i^K (or Phi = |w| if |l| != |r|) satisfies Phi(v) = Phi(u) + delta for every step u -> v. Hence all derivations between two words have equal length, and a terminating one-rule system is confluent iff it has random descent.

- **assumptions:** binary rules with |l|, |r| <= 5 and ternary rules with |l|, |r| <= 3 for the potential; length-preserving binary rules with bordered lhs, |l| = 4, 5, words <= 10 for confluence/RD
- **methodology:**

  Construct (c, K, delta) as in the proof (first letter whose position power sums differ, least K); check Phi(v) - Phi(u) = delta on every one-step pair from every word up to length 7. Separately compare confluence and random descent on exhaustive derivation graphs.

- **falsification:** A rule with no admissible (c, K), a step violating the constant change, or a confluent length-preserving rule without random descent.
- **summary:**
  - constant-step potential verified for 5365 rules on 470927 one-step pairs: 0 violations (largest exponent K needed: 2)
  - length-preserving bordered rules |l| = 4, 5: 770 tested, 94 confluent (all of them length-uniform: True), 220 length-uniform, of which 126 are not confluent
- **analysis:**

  The constant-step potential of Theorem 2 exists and works for all 5365 tested rules (0 violations on 470,927 steps, K <= 2); among 770 bordered length-preserving rules with |l| = 4, 5 every confluent rule is length-uniform, while 126 are length-uniform without being confluent.

- **verification:** PROVEN
- **related:** CLM-C04-06
- **runs:** 3; last 2026-10-18T09:19:28Z (4.653 s), results sha256 `31cca82d9f4c7cee…`, code `experiments/C04/E06_step_potential.py`

### EXP-C04-07: Rewriting games: parity for random-descent rules, run decomposition for unary left-hand sides

- **cycle:** C04
- **agent:** Mathematical Explorer
- **question:** What do the Sprague-Grundy values of rewriting games look like?
- **hypothesis:**

  H-C04-4: for random-descent rules g(w) = D(w) mod 2 (D the unique derivation length), so P-positions are exactly the words with even D (for ab -> ba: an even number of inversions). H-C04-5: for a rule a^k -> u the game is the disjoint sum of a take-and-break game on the maximal a-runs, g(w) = XOR over runs of G(run length). H-C04-6 (bold): every rewriting game in the family has Grundy values <= 3 on words of length <= 10.

- **assumptions:** the 162 terminating rules; all words of length <= 10 (<= 8 for the doubling rules)
- **methodology:**

  Grundy values by mex over the exact derivation graph. For unary left-hand sides the run game G is computed independently from its definition (heap n, rewrite position i, resulting heaps i + e_0, e_1, ..., n - k - i + e_t from the a-blocks e_j of u).

- **falsification:** A random-descent word with g != D mod 2; a word whose Grundy value differs from the XOR of run values; a Grundy value >= 4.
- **summary:** 162 rewriting games; random-descent rules violating g = D mod 2: 0; unary-lhs rules checked against the run decomposition: 58, failures: 0; histogram of max Grundy value per rule: {1: 102, 3: 60}; highest: [(['aa', 'b'], 3), (['aa', 'ab'], 3), (['aa', 'ba'], 3), (['aa', 'bb'], 3), (['aa', 'abb'], 3)]
- **analysis:** Parity theorem holds for all RD rules; unary-lhs games decompose over a-runs for all 58 such rules; maximal Grundy value 3 on words of length <= 10 (60 rules).
- **related:** CLM-C04-08; CLM-C04-13
- **runs:** 1; last 2026-10-18T08:01:26Z (33.458 s), results sha256 `c3e4b5f1b21dcfdc…`, code `experiments/C04/E07_rewriting_games.py`

### EXP-C04-08: Unary rewriting games = octal games: Dawson's Kayles (aa -> b), Officers (aa -> ab) and others

- **cycle:** C04
- **agent:** Historian and Novelty Analyst
- **question:** Which classical take-and-break games are the rewriting games of rules a^k -> u?
- **hypothesis:**

  H-C04-7: on the word a^n, the game of aa -> b (also bb, bbb, bab) is Dawson's Kayles 0.07; aa -> ab (also ba, abb, bba) is Officers 0.6; aaa -> b (bb, bbb, bab) is 0.007; aaa -> ab (ba, abb, bba) is 0.06; aaa -> aba is 0.4. H-C04-6 (bold, from EXP-C04-07): Grundy values of rewriting games in the family never exceed 3.

- **assumptions:** run decomposition (EXP-C04-07); Guy-Smith periodicity theorem for finite octal games
- **methodology:**

  (1) Grundy value of the single word a^n from its exact derivation graph (no decomposition), n <= 20, compared with the octal recursion and with the generic run-game recursion. (2) Octal Grundy values to n = 3000; smallest period p <= 300 with preperiod n0 holding over the whole range, and the Guy-Smith window n0 <= n < 2 n0 + p + t checked explicitly.

- **falsification:** Any n <= 20 where the word game, the run game and the octal recursion disagree; a Grundy value >= 4 refutes H-C04-6.
- **summary:** word game = run game for n <= 20 and run game = octal recursion for n <= 3000: True; aa->b (Dawson's Kayles): period 34 from n0=53, max G 9, first G>=4 at n=14; aa->bb (Dawson's Kayles): period 34 from n0=53, max G 9, first G>=4 at n=14; aa->bab (Dawson's Kayles): period 34 from n0=53, max G 9, first G>=4 at n=14; aa->ab (Officers): period None (none <= 300 up to n=3000), max G 91, first G>=4 at n=11; aa->abb (Officers): period None (none <= 300 up to n=3000), max G 91, first G>=4 at n=11; aa->ba (Officers): period None (none <= 300 up to n=3000), max G 91, first G>=4 at n=11; aaa->b (octal 0.007): period None (none <= 300 up to n=3000), max G 120, first G>=4 at n=15; aaa->bab (octal 0.007): period None (none <= 300 up to n=3000), max G 120, first G>=4 at n=15; aaa->ab (octal 0.06): period None (none <= 300 up to n=3000), max G 117, first G>=4 at n=15; aaa->aba (octal 0.4): period 34 from n0=54, max G 9, first G>=4 at n=15; aaa->aab (not octal (left heap >= 2)): period 4 from n0=2, max G 3, first G>=4 at n=None
- **analysis:**

  Unary rewriting games coincide with octal games (Dawson's Kayles, Officers, 0.007, 0.06, 0.4); Dawson's Kayles and 0.4 have period 34 (Guy-Smith window verified); Officers, 0.007, 0.06 show no period <= 300 up to n = 3000; a^14 has Grundy value 4 under aa -> b.

- **counterexamples:** aa -> b: Grundy value of a^14 is 4 (H-C04-6 refuted)

### EXP-C04-10: D(n+2) for aab -> ba equals A247983(n) = least k with log 2 - sum_{h<=k} 1/(h 2^h) < 2^-n

- **cycle:** C04
- **agent:** Historian and Novelty Analyst
- **question:** Is the OEIS match of EXP-C04-09 an identity or a coincidence of 60 terms?
- **hypothesis:** H-C04-8: A247983(n) = n - J(n) with J(n) = max{j >= 0 : 2^j + j <= n + 1}, hence A247983(n) = D(n + 2) for all n >= 1 (D the derivational complexity of aab -> ba).
- **assumptions:** A247983 as defined in the OEIS (name field, cached); mpmath with n + 80 bits of precision
- **methodology:**

  For n = 1..1500 compute the least k with log(2) - sum_{h=1..k} 1/(h 2^h) < 2^-n in high-precision arithmetic (with a margin check that the decisive comparisons are far from the rounding level), and compare with n - J(n) and with D(n + 2).

- **falsification:** Any n with a mismatch.
- **summary:** A247983(n) computed for n = 1..1500 with n+80 bits: mismatches with n - J(n) or D(n+2): 0; closest decisive comparison is 2^-19.0 away in relative terms (precision n+80 bits)
- **analysis:** A247983(n) = n - max{j : 2^j + j <= n + 1} = D(n + 2) for all n <= 1500 in high-precision arithmetic; the identity is proved in proofs/paper/C04.md Theorem 8.
- **verification:** PROVEN
- **related:** CLM-C04-10

### EXP-C05-01: Spectrum census of two-variable identities t = x (2-4 operations), sizes 1-6

- **cycle:** C05
- **agent:** Mathematical Explorer
- **question:** Which sizes of finite magmas can satisfy a single invented identity t(x, y) = x?
- **hypothesis:** H-C05-1 (bold): every identity t = x has spectrum {1} or all positive integers - single identities cannot carve out arithmetic restrictions on the size of a model.
- **assumptions:**
  - System B: magmas without any assumed law
  - identities up to renaming of variables
  - 'nontrivial' = x occurs in t but is neither its leftmost nor its rightmost leaf (otherwise a projection is a model of every size, or x is absent and only n = 1 works)
  - SAT (CaDiCaL via pysat), conflict budget 1e6 per instance
- **methodology:**

  For each nontrivial identity: SAT model existence for n = 1..6; exhaustive model enumeration with isomorphism rejection for n <= 3; record whether all enumerated models are quasigroups. Mirror images (t read right-to-left) are paired; their spectra must agree. (Larger n are enumerated, and n = 7..10 decided, only for restricted identities in EXP-C05-02.)

- **falsification:** Any identity whose spectrum within 1..6 is neither {1} nor everything refutes H-C05-1.
- **summary:**
  - 115 nontrivial identities; spectrum patterns over n=1..6: {'100000': 2, '100010': 14, '100110': 6, '10011?': 1, '101000': 2, '101011': 4, '101100': 6, '101110': 16, '101111': 4, '110110': 8, '111111': 52}
  - identities with a restricted spectrum (neither {1} nor all): 61
  - mirror pairs with different spectra (must be 0): 0
- **analysis:**

  All 115 nontrivial two-variable identities t = x with 2-4 operations were decided for n = 1..6 except law 167 at n = 6 (UNKNOWN within budget; excluded by the Lean theorem). 61 have restricted spectra, 52 are full on 1..6, 2 admit only n = 1 there; mirror pairs always agree; 18 identities have non-quasigroup models of order <= 3.

- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **novelty:** REDISCOVERY
- **related:** CLM-C05-01; CLM-C05-02; CLM-C05-03
- **runs:** 1; last 2026-10-18T08:07:36Z (154.555 s), results sha256 `607a0e46158d394b…`, code `experiments/C05/E01_two_variable_census.py`

### EXP-C05-02: Restricted-spectrum identities: extended spectra (n = 7, 8) and forced structure

- **cycle:** C05
- **agent:** Mathematical Explorer
- **question:** For identities whose spectrum is restricted, which classical laws hold in all their small models, and how do the spectra continue beyond n = 7?
- **hypothesis:**

  Restricted spectra come from identities that force quasigroups with extra laws (idempotence, semisymmetry, total symmetry), i.e. classical combinatorial designs; their spectra continue with the corresponding design spectra.

- **assumptions:**
  - input: the restricted identities found by EXP-C05-01 (read from results/EXP-C05-01.json)
  - SAT conflict budget 5e5 per instance for n = 7, 8 (UNKNOWN recorded as such)
  - sound implied constraints (Lemma 1/4 of proofs/paper/C05.md): rows are permutations when t = y*(...), columns are permutations when t = (...)*y; symmetry break 0*0 in {0,1}
- **methodology:**

  Extend each spectrum to n = 7, 8. Enumerate all models up to isomorphism for n <= 5 (cap 5000 per size) and test a battery of classical laws on every model; group identities with identical model sets for n <= 5 (candidate equivalent laws).

- **falsification:** A restricted identity with a non-quasigroup model, or spectra that do not match any design-type pattern.
- **summary:**
  - 63 identities studied; spectrum patterns over n = 1..8: {'1000001?': 2, '10001000': 2, '10001011': 8, '1000101?': 4, '10011001': 2, '10011011': 2, '100110??': 2, '10011??1': 1, '1010001?': 2, '10101111': 4, '10110010': 4, '10110011': 2, '10111011': 16, '10111111': 4, '11011011': 8}
  - all small models quasigroups for 52/63
  - 58 groups of identities with identical model sets for n <= 5
- **analysis:**

  The 63 non-full identities fall into 15 spectrum patterns on n = 1..8, reproducing every spectrum fragment stated in the ETP notes for the laws concerned; 52/63 have only quasigroup models for n <= 5; the Dupont family (16 identities incl. laws 63, 115) has a single, medial model class at each of n = 3, 4, 5. The 'identical model set' grouping in this run compared non-canonical representatives and is not used (defect recorded).

- **verification:** COMPUTATIONALLY SUPPORTED
- **related:** CLM-C05-02

### EXP-C05-03: Frontier push: spectra of open order-4 laws for n = 2..10 (SAT with sound Latin-row constraints)

- **cycle:** C05
- **agent:** Formal Verifier
- **question:** For the order-4 laws x = t whose spectrum is listed as unknown in the Equational Theories Project notes, which sizes n <= 12 admit models?
- **hypothesis:** Several open laws have unresolved small sizes that SAT can decide; our results must agree with every membership/non-membership already stated in the notes.
- **assumptions:**
  - laws and partial knowledge taken from B. Le Floch's ETP notes 'spectrum.pdf' (Lean Zulip)
  - for laws of the form x = y*(...), every left translation is onto, hence a bijection in a finite magma, so 'every row is a permutation' is added as a sound implied constraint
  - symmetry breaking 0*0 in {0,1} (any magma can be relabelled so)
  - CaDiCaL conflict budget 1e6 per instance; UNKNOWN is reported as such
- **methodology:** SAT model search for each law and n = 2..10; every SAT answer is re-verified by evaluating the law on the returned table; results are compared with the sizes stated in the notes.
- **falsification:** A disagreement with a size stated in the notes would indicate a bug (ours or theirs).
- **summary:** 467: in [5, 7, 8, 9], out [2, 3, 4, 6], unknown [10]; 501: in [4, 5, 8, 9], out [2, 3, 6, 7], unknown [10]; 670: in [4, 5], out [2, 3, 6, 7], unknown [8, 9, 10]; 677: in [5, 7, 9], out [2, 3, 4, 6], unknown [8, 10]; 704: in [5, 7, 8], out [2, 3, 4, 6], unknown [9, 10]; 883: in [2, 4, 5, 7, 8, 10], out [3, 6], unknown [9]; 907: in [3, 7], out [2, 4, 5, 6], unknown [8, 9, 10]; 1076: in [5], out [2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8], unknown [9, 10]; 1083: in [3, 4, 7, 8, 9], out [2, 5, 6], unknown [10]; 1110: in [4, 5, 7, 8, 9], out [2, 3, 6], unknown [10]; 1279: in [5, 7, 8], out [2, 3, 4, 6], unknown [9, 10]; 1286: in [7, 9], out [2, 3, 4, 5, 6], unknown [8, 10]; 1313: in [5, 7, 9], out [2, 3, 4, 6], unknown [8, 10]; 1516: in [5, 7, 8, 9], out [2, 3, 4, 6], unknown [10]; 1719: in [3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10], out [2], unknown []; disagreements with the notes: 0
- **analysis:**

  15 laws with unknown spectrum in the ETP notes decided for n <= 10 except a few sizes (UNKNOWN at budget 1e6); zero disagreements with the notes. The field decided_beyond_notes compares only with the sizes in each law's own paragraph of the notes; the notes' lists of laws without models of order 2 (or 3) and their Gaussian-integer models of law 467 cover several of those sizes, so CLM-C05-07/08 and the README frontier table list only facts absent from the notes.

- **related:** CLM-C05-07; CLM-C05-08; CLM-C05-09

### EXP-C05-05: Bold hypothesis H-C05-A: full spectrum iff affine models over every Z_n

- **cycle:** C05
- **agent:** Mathematical Inventor
- **question:** Is having models of every order the same as having affine models x*y = a x + b y + c (mod n) for every n?
- **hypothesis:** H-C05-A (bold): a nontrivial identity t = x has models of all orders 1..6 iff for every n <= 6 it has an affine model over Z_n; restricted spectra are exactly the affine obstructions.
- **assumptions:** identities and spectra from EXP-C05-01 (n <= 6); exhaustive search over (a, b, c) in Z_n^3
- **methodology:** For every identity, list the orders n <= 6 having an affine model over Z_n; compare with the SAT spectrum.
- **falsification:** A full-spectrum identity without an affine model for some n (refutes 'only if'), or an affine model of an order outside the SAT spectrum (would indicate a bug).
- **summary:** 52 identities with spectrum 1..6 full; 4 of them lack an affine model for some order <= 6 (each refutes H-C05-A); restricted identities whose SAT spectrum contains orders without affine models: 39; consistency bugs: 0
- **analysis:**

  H-C05-A refuted - four identities (two mirror pairs) have models of every order 1..6 but affine models over Z_n only for n in {1,5} or {1,2,5}; 39 restricted identities also have spectrum members without affine models; no affine model was ever found outside the SAT spectrum.

- **counterexamples:** x = (y*x)*(x*(y*y)): NAND on {0,1} is a model, no affine operation over Z_2 is
